# End-to-End Backlog — the Personal Agent Platform

> **Status:** Review draft (not finalized) · **Date:** 2026-11-14 · **Tasks:** 512 across 15 modules (479 original + 33 from a gap-coverage audit).
> **Reads with:** [current-state.md](current-state.md) · [gap-analysis.md](gap-analysis.md) · [target-architecture.md](target-architecture.md) · [implementation-roadmap.md](implementation-roadmap.md).
> **This is a plan. No code has been changed.** Built by a 16-agent module breakdown, then **gap-audited by a 15-agent coverage pass** (one auditor per plan slice) that cross-checked every roadmap / target-architecture / gap-analysis item against the full task list; the 33 tasks it found missing are merged in (marked ✚) and its ownership/sequencing resolutions are in §5.

## 0. Locked decisions (the frame for every task)

1. **Consolidate** `agents/` + `platform/` into `legacy/` — `server/`+`client/` is the single source of truth.
2. **`whatsapp-web.js` (QR) only** for agent replies **and** outbound. Official **Cloud API / BROADCAST path is out**; outbound = the **Trackly-style QR drip** (accepted-risk, secondary number, throttle+window+jitter, opt-out, send-ledger, per-agent send mutex). *(OTP keeps the Meta Cloud API purely as a platform auth sender.)* A CI guard (QA-58) mechanically forbids any Cloud-API agent transport, flight, or LinkedIn surface from reappearing.
3. **Standard scenarios only:** WhatsApp business agent (harden), lead-gen/outbound drip, appointment booking, website offer/price monitoring, structural multi-agent. **Flight and LinkedIn DM are out.**
4. **North star — anyone builds any personal agent, easily.** The **Agent Creation Studio** (STU) is the centerpiece; the audit added the pieces that make it work for *any* agent type, not just reply bots (STU-50 non-reply specs, STU-51 simulate-anything, STU-52 one funnel, STU-53 member access, STU-54 capability-flag UI).
5. **Modular-monolith-first**, Postgres backbone (queue via `SKIP LOCKED` + node-cron, pgvector later). **Skip** Temporal/Kafka/Google-A2A/LangGraph/external-IdP; in-process tool registry over a full MCP mesh. Everything **additive** around the single-turn reply loop (`executionMode:'reply'` stays the byte-for-byte default).

## 1. How to read this backlog

- **Task ID** = `MODULE-NN` (prefixes in §3b). **✚** marks a task added by the gap-audit. **Type** ∈ UX · FE · BE · DB · API · Infra · Security · Test · Docs. **Phase** maps to [implementation-roadmap.md](implementation-roadmap.md). **Pri** = P0 (do-first / fixes a live hole) → P3 (aspirational).
- §2 north-star flow · §3 roll-ups + module index · §4 tasks by module · **§5 gap-audit result (additions + ownership + sequencing)** · §6 completeness-critic addendum · §7 execution guidance.
- Phase/priority labels are **relative**, not calendar commitments. Work phase-by-phase.

## 2. The north star — the easiest path to a personal agent

```
Describe it in words ("reply to my salon's WhatsApp, answer prices, book appointments")
   │  (or: pick a role/vertical template, or paste a link / upload a doc)
   ▼
Plain-English DRAFT  →  review & edit   (validated AgentSpec — never code; never invents facts)
   │  the NL builder can now produce reply OR schedule / outbound / booking / monitor agents (STU-50)
   ▼
Pick (optional, sane defaults):  what it DOES (tools) · WHEN (on message / schedule / webhook) ·
   WHICH channel · what needs MY approval
   ▼
SIMULATE any agent — reply, agentic, or scheduled — dry-run, sends nothing (STU-51)  →  go live
   ▼
Edit anytime (next message) · PUBLISH a version · ROLL BACK · CLONE
```
One unified funnel (STU-52): describe, template, and "what should this agent do?" all converge on the same `AgentSpec` → `applySpec()` write-path, so "generic" and "easy" are one mechanism. See module **STU** (§4) + **UX**.

## 4. Tasks by module

### 4.1  Foundation, Security Hardening & Legacy Consolidation (Phase 0)  `FND`  ·  36 tasks

Phase 0 closes the exploitable live holes on the deployed instance (unauth /ws QR+message leak, unauth unbounded-spend widget, SSRF in learn.ts, plaintext Telegram botToken + bearer tokens, reflecting CORS, unwired Razorpay webhook + prod free-upgrade), restores the lost per-conversation serialization lock that causes double-replies, makes builds deterministic, and collapses the three-generation (agents/ + platform/) and doc drift into one source of truth. Every task is additive with ZERO schema changes (encryption/hash are in-place value changes) so it ships immediately and in parallel with Phase 1 migrations. The hard gate is that mock-askClaude regression tests for the money/safety path (auto-send, NEEDS_HUMAN, [[ALERT]], {{LINK}}) land BEFORE any runtime refactor, and the WS-auth + token-hash changes ship with their matching client change in the same release.

Update or remove the root package.json `whatsapp` script path. **Done:** server/ and client/ build and `npm test` pass unchanged; grep shows no server/ or client/ file imports anything under legacy/; `npm run whatsapp` either runs from the new legacy path or is removed with a one-line note; git history preserved via move. |

No schema change. **Done:** A widget request with no or wrong key is rejected (401); a request with the correct derived key works; the key is verified with a constant-time compare. |

| **FND-15** | SSRF allowlist in learn.ts (incl. redirects) | Security | 0 | P0 | — | POST /agents/:id/training/learn server-fetches an arbitrary user URL with redirect:follow and no host filtering. Add lib/ssrfGuard.ts that resolves + validates the host before every hop and follows redirects manually, rejecting loopback, private, link-local and 169.254.169.254 metadata targets; wire it into fetchHtml. **Done:** learn-from-link refuses http://127.0.0.1, http://169.254.169.254, RFC1918 and link-local hosts AND a public URL that 30x-redirects to any of them; a normal public website still ingests; friendly owner error on refusal. |
| **FND-16** | AES-256-GCM crypto module (lib/crypto.ts) | Security | 0 | P0 | — | Add an app-level AES-256-GCM encrypt/decrypt helper keyed from an env secret with a keyId prefix for future rotation, producing a self-describing ciphertext string that fits existing String columns (no schema change). This is the shared primitive for secret-at-rest tasks. **Done:** encrypt(decrypt(x))===x; ciphertext carries keyId + iv + auth tag; a tampered ciphertext fails to decrypt; missing key fails fast at boot with a clear message. |
| **FND-17** | Encrypt Telegram botToken in place | Security | 0 | P0 | FND-16 | Telegram botToken is stored plaintext inside Agent.channelConfig JSON. Encrypt it with lib/crypto on the write path (agents PATCH channelConfig) and decrypt on the read path (telegram.ts botToken()), transparently migrating any existing plaintext value on next write/read. No schema change. **Done:** A newly saved Telegram token is unreadable at rest; the Telegram adapter still long-polls/sends with the decrypted value; a pre-existing plaintext token keeps working and is upgraded to ciphertext. |
| **FND-18** | Hash bearer tokens at rest | Security | 0 | P0 | — | User.token is a plaintext opaque bearer token looked up on every request. Store only a SHA-256 hash of the token (the opaque value handed to the client is unchanged), look up by hash in authMiddleware, and migrate existing plaintext tokens via a short dual-read grace window / lazy re-hash on next login. No schema change (same String column). **Done:** The DB column holds only hashes for new/logged-in users; existing clients stay authenticated during the grace window then carry a hash; a stolen DB row cannot be replayed as a bearer token. |
| **FND-19** | Non-reflecting CORS in production | Security | 0 | P0 | — | app.ts falls back to cors({origin:true}) (reflects any origin) when CORS_ORIGIN is unset. In production require an explicit CORS_ORIGIN allowlist and fail closed (no reflection); keep the permissive default only for dev/test. **Done:** With NODE_ENV=production and no CORS_ORIGIN the server refuses to reflect arbitrary origins (startup error or deny-by-default); configured origins are allowed; dev/test behavior unchanged. |
| **FND-20** | Wire the Razorpay webhook (server-authoritative billing) | API | 0 | P0 | — | verifyWebhookSignature exists but is never wired, so a paid checkout whose browser never calls /verify leaves the org un-upgraded. Add a raw-body POST /api/webhooks/razorpay route (mounted before express.json, unauthenticated but HMAC-verified) that idempotently applies the plan on payment.captured/subscription events. **Done:** A validly signed webhook upgrades the org even without the client /verify call; an invalid signature is rejected (400); replaying the same event does not double-apply; raw body is preserved for the HMAC. |
| **FND-21** | Block /subscribe free-upgrade in production | Security | 0 | P0 | — | POST /api/billing/subscribe free-upgrades any plan when NODE_ENV!=='production' OR Razorpay is unconfigured. Add an in-process Map<convKey,Promise> chain keyed by agentId+contact that serializes processInbound per conversation. **Done:** Two inbound messages from the same contact within <1s produce exactly one AI reply across WhatsApp AND Telegram; different conversations still process concurrently; no deadlock on error (lock always releases). |

ConnectCard.tsx:36 builds the embed with data-agent only; once FND-12 gates the public widget endpoints on a signed key, the owner has no server-provided way to obtain that key and the gated widget becomes non-installable. This is the BE/API seam between FND-12 (public validation) and FND-14/FND-51 (snippet + install UX) that no inventory task owns. **Done:** An authenticated owner retrieves their website agent's signed widget key; the returned/embeddable snippet includes it; a different org cannot retrieve another org's key; the key validates against the FND-12 public-endpoint check; rotating the widget-signing secret invalidates previously issued snippets. |

**UX coverage —** AgentDetail.tsx + ConnectCard.tsx live QR/status over WebSocket must keep working after /ws requires a token (no blank QR, no silent dead socket); Website chat-widget install flow: ConnectCard embed snippet must now carry the signed widget key, with a copy button and a 'what changed' confirmation, staying within the invisible-UI click budget; Embedded widget.js chat bubble on the customer's site must send the key and keep polling/replying unchanged; Graceful re-auth/logout UX after the bearer-token-hash rollout (grace window so existing sessions are not hard-logged-out); No regression to the zero-training SetupGuide / 'invisible UI' agent-creation flow while hardening runs underneath it; Owner billing/subscription screen behavior when /subscribe free-upgrade is blocked in prod (clear 'use checkout to pay' path, no dead button).

**Open questions —**
- Widget key as a STATELESS HMAC (HMAC(WIDGET_SIGNING_SECRET, agentId)) keeps Phase 0 schema-free but is not individually rotatable; is deferring a per-agent stored+rotatable widgetKey column to Phase 1 acceptable?
- Bearer-token hardening in Phase 0 is hash-at-rest ONLY (lookup by hash); token expiry + rotate-on-login + logout are deferred to Phase 3 per the roadmap - confirm that split.
- Migrating existing plaintext bearer tokens: lazy re-hash on next login with a short dual-read grace window vs. a one-time forced re-login - which is acceptable for the live user base?
- OTP delivery still uses the Meta WhatsApp Cloud API as a platform-level sender (auth infra, not an agent channel). Phase 0 leaves it as-is; flagging only as a minor note - confirm no change wanted.
- Legacy prototype (agents/) after the move to legacy/: keep `npm run whatsapp` runnable from the new path, or drop the root script + its root deps (@anthropic-ai/sdk, qrcode-terminal, whatsapp-web.js) entirely?
- Should the TEMPORARY /test-send smoke route in the agents router be removed now, or kept (owner-gated + env-gated) until the Phase 5 outbound-drip handler lands?


### 4.2  Database, Migrations & Multi-tenancy  `DB`  ·  24 tasks

This module replaces the destructive `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` boot-time schema sync (which runs against the live Railway Postgres on every deploy) with a baselined Prisma migration history applied via `prisma migrate deploy`, guarded by CI. It then hardens tenant isolation by backfilling `orgId` (+indexes) onto the four tables that today isolate only transitively via `agentId` (`ChannelSession`, `TrainingProfile`, `TrainingSource`, `Message`), adds the two missing hot-path indexes, and establishes a seed/dev-reset story, a documented DR restore drill, and a recorded (deferred) RLS plan. Everything is additive around the existing 11-model Prisma spine and keeps handlers backend-agnostic across the SQLite-dev / Postgres-prod divergence.

Record the decision that PostgreSQL migrations are the single source of truth for prod, and how dev schema changes are mirrored (keep SQLite + `db push` as a fast path, or add a local Postgres). This is the foundational decision that unblocks the baseline and the entrypoint switch. **Done:** A short decision record in docs/ (e.g. docs/ops/MIGRATIONS.md) states the chosen provider-of-record, how dev authors/validates migrations against Postgres, and what happens to the Dockerfile sed line; reviewers can follow it to land DB-3 and DB-5 without ambiguity. |

This is the safe target to diff/baseline against so the first migration cannot silently drop prod columns. **Done:** A reachable shadow DB whose `prisma migrate diff` against the current prod introspection is empty; SHADOW_DATABASE_URL documented in server/.env.example and server/.env.production.example and consumed by the datasource block. |

This is the hard unlock for every later table. **Done:** `prisma migrate diff --from-migrations prisma/migrations --to-schema-datamodel prisma/schema.prisma --shadow-database-url $SHADOW_DATABASE_URL` reports no drift; `migrate deploy` on a fresh clone reproduces prod schema byte-for-byte; prod shows 0_init as applied with zero column drops. |

An up-to-date DB is a no-op; a pending migration applies forward-only. **Done:** Prod boot runs `prisma migrate deploy` (never `db push`/`--accept-data-loss`/`--force-reset`); a pending migration applies on deploy; an up-to-date DB starts with no DDL; a failed migration fails the deploy loudly. |

Distinct from DB-3 (DDL-only on a fresh clone) and DB-17/QA-48 (DR integrity restore). Required by the roadmap Phase-1 risk and resolves the open maintenance-window question. **Done:** A dated rehearsal report records, for a prod-scale restored clone: measured duration of each orgId batched backfill (ChannelSession/TrainingProfile/TrainingSource/Message), the acquired lock mode + hold time for each NOT NULL enforcement, and that Message CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY completes without an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock; the maintenance-window question is resolved (needed yes/no + size) and the Message backfill batch size is validated before running against live prod. |

The specific hazard: DB-11's non-transactional CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY on failure leaves an INVALID index AND Prisma marks the migration failed, blocking every subsequent deploy until an operator intervenes. No inventory task covers this (DB-3 authors the baseline, DB-17 is DR data-loss recovery, DB-20 documents only the happy path). Cover diagnosis, dropping the invalid index, 'migrate resolve --rolled-back', fix + re-run, and when to escalate to the DR restore. **Done:** docs/ops/MIGRATIONS.md (or DR_RUNBOOK.md) contains a step-by-step recovery for a failed/partial prod migration — including the INVALID CONCURRENT-index case and the 'migrate resolve --rolled-back' unblock — and a reviewer can follow it to restore deploys after a deliberately-failed migration on the shadow/clone with zero data loss. |

**UX coverage —** Conversations / lead inbox (client ChatsPanel) + message-history load latency — gated by the new Message(conversationId,createdAt) and Message.orgId indexes; Analytics dashboard counts (replies today / leads by status) — gated by the new UsageEvent(orgId,createdAt) index; Agent connect / QR status surface — unaffected in behavior but ChannelSession gains orgId (writes in whatsapp.ts setStatus); Developer onboarding flow (DEV.md first-run) — new seed + dev-reset commands replace the raw `prisma db push` step; No new end-user screens: this module is backend/DB/infra; its only user-visible effect is faster inbox/analytics loads and safer deploys.

**Open questions —**
- Dev dialect strategy: keep SQLite + `db push` as the zero-setup dev fast-path (migrations authored/validated only against Postgres), or move dev to a local Postgres (new docker-compose service) for true migration parity? Prisma migration SQL is dialect-specific, so this decision shapes DB-1/DB-3/DB-6/DB-16.
- Can Railway give us a safe structural clone of prod (DB branch or backup-restore to a scratch instance) to baseline and diff against without touching the live DB? Needed for DB-2/DB-3.
- Is a brief maintenance window acceptable for enforcing Message.orgId NOT NULL + building the Message indexes on the live prod table, even with CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY and batched backfill? (DB-10/DB-11)
- OTP currently uses the Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender (auth infra, not an agent channel) and is unaffected by this module — flagging only as a minor note per the locked decision; no DB change proposed.


### 4.3  Model Gateway, Runtime & Per-tenant Cost Control  `MGW`  ·  21 tasks

This module turns the single hardcoded askClaude call into a provider-abstracted model gateway (timeout, bounded retry on 429/5xx, token+cost capture) behind a runtime facade that dispatches executionMode 'reply' (byte-identical default) vs a forward-declared 'agentic' sibling, threads an executionId+agentVersion trace onto an extended UsageEvent, and adds a per-tenant daily message/cost budget gate enforced in both processInbound and the public widget before any model call. It also makes the system prompt channel-aware (today it hardcodes "WhatsApp"), keeps {{LINK}}/[[ALERT]]/NEEDS_HUMAN/away-once as deterministic post-processing, and gives owners a calm client surface for usage, cost and limits. Every change is additive around the proven reply loop so nothing that works today regresses.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

It becomes the single LLM chokepoint, capturing res.usage that brain.ts currently discards (brain.ts lines 20-26). Anthropic is the only provider wired now, but the signature is provider-neutral. **Done:** callModel returns text + exact input/output token counts + stopReason for a real Anthropic call; a unit test with a mocked SDK asserts usage is captured (not discarded) and refusal maps to empty text. |

Map SDK errors to typed codes (rate_limited, overloaded, timeout, refusal, bad_request) returned to callers so the engine can choose a safe fallback. **Done:** A simulated 429-then-200 yields one successful result after exactly one retry; a 400 does not retry; a timeout aborts within the configured budget and surfaces errorCode='timeout'. |

Call canSpend(orgId) at the top of processInbound after agent load and before the gateway call, returning a new 'budget_exceeded' outcome with zero model calls, and add 'budget_exceeded' to ProcessResult.status and OBS-8's enumerated outcome set. This is what target-architecture §11 ('checked in processInbound AND the public widget') and MGW-18's assertion actually require. **Done:** An inbound WhatsApp/Telegram message for an org past its daily cap makes zero model calls, returns a safe status, and records exactly one 'budget_exceeded' trace row; under the cap the reply path is byte-identical; 'budget_exceeded' is present in ProcessResult.status and counted as one row by the OBS-8 one-row-per-run test. |

**UX coverage —** Agents home (client/src/components/AgentsStats.tsx) gains a per-tenant usage/limit meter: 'Replies today X / daily cap Y' with a calm progress bar; Subscription page (client/src/pages/Subscription.tsx) shows usage + indicative cost-to-date against the plan's daily cap, with an outcome-named nudge near the limit; A near-cap / over-cap plain-language state ('Your agents have replied to today's limit — replies resume tomorrow or upgrade') following show-what-changed, no raw numbers dump; An owner-only 'What your agents did' execution/cost feed (new) behind one named disclosure, reusing the invisible-UI card style; empty state carries a first action; Role-gating: usage/cost/execution surfaces are owner-visible and hidden for members; Website-agent replies read as 'website chat' rather than 'WhatsApp' once the prompt is channel-aware (visible to the owner in the Test Console).

**Open questions —**
- Default daily message cap and daily cost cap per plan (free/growth/pro) — plans.ts has only maxAgents today; need the owner's numbers (or a sensible default like free=200 msgs/day) before the budget gate can ship with real limits.
- Should owners see indicative INR cost at all, or only a message count against the cap? Cost transparency helps trust but a fluctuating 'indicative' number may confuse tier-2 owners.
- Pricing map is indicative only; confirm it is acceptable to store exact input/output token counts on UsageEvent and re-price historically rather than freezing a price at write time.
- Confirmed out of scope but flagging per locked decision 2: OTP delivery still uses Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender; the gateway is model-only and does not touch that path — no change intended.


### 4.4  Observability & Evaluation  `OBS`  ·  30 tasks

This module turns the single-turn reply loop from a blind counter into a fully traceable, evaluable system without changing its behavior. It captures token/model/cost/latency at the askClaude chokepoint, persists exactly one fire-and-forget execution-trace row per processInbound across every outcome by extending UsageEvent additively, adds pino structured logging with redaction, and exposes owner-only execution/metrics/readiness endpoints plus an owner-facing execution viewer and dashboard metric tiles. Everything is additive around the working reply loop and the existing analytics page; it stays pino-only (OTel deferred to Phase 7) and introduces no new infra beyond Postgres and (later) node-cron.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **OBS-1** | Extend UsageEvent additively into an execution-trace row | DB | 2 | P0 | DB module (Phase 1 migrations / orgId backfill) | Add nullable columns to the existing UsageEvent model so one row can describe a full run: executionId, agentVersion, model, provider, inputTokens, outputTokens, costMicros, latencyMs, outcome, trigger, conversationId, errorCode, approvalState. Keep the table name UsageEvent and the existing id/orgId/agentId/type/createdAt columns so current analytics counting keeps working. **Done:** A Prisma migration adds all listed columns as nullable with no backfill required; existing UsageEvent rows and the current /api/analytics counts are unaffected; `prisma migrate` runs clean against a shadow DB and no `db push --accept-data-loss` is used. |
| **OBS-2** | Add indexes for execution-trace query patterns | DB | 2 | P1 | OBS-1 | Add the indexes the new read endpoints need: a unique index on executionId, (agentId, createdAt), and (orgId, outcome, createdAt). Complements the Phase 1 UsageEvent(orgId, createdAt) index rather than duplicating it. **Done:** Listing executions by org/agent/outcome and the metrics aggregation use an index (no full-table scan on the growth table); executionId is unique so a trace write cannot duplicate a run. |

Keep a backward-compatible text accessor (or update the three callers: engine.processInbound, engine.draftFor, learn.ts) so reply output text is byte-identical. Coordinate the shape with the Runtime/Gateway module so this becomes the shared model-gateway contract rather than a competing refactor. **Done:** askClaude surfaces inputTokens/outputTokens/model/latencyMs/stopReason from the Anthropic response; the reply text for a fixed input is byte-identical to today; refusal handling (stop_reason==='refusal' -> '') is preserved; all callers compile and existing unit tests stay green. |

**Open questions —**
- Metric semantics need owner sign-off: which outcomes count as 'success' vs 'intervention' (e.g. is 'away' a success? is every 'draft' an intervention since the owner must act, or only skipped/edited drafts?). OBS-13/OBS-29 assume approvalState (OBS-16) distinguishes real human action.
- Retention policy: default window (90d?) and whether to prune Message rows at all — pruning Message would shrink inbox/conversation history users can see, so the safer default may be to prune only trace rows and keep (or separately archive) Messages.
- Scheduler host: the retention sweep ideally runs on the Phase 5 node-cron ticker; until that lands, is a bounded setInterval / boot-time sweep acceptable, or should retention wait for Phase 5?
- Pricing map is indicative only and will drift as Anthropic prices change; confirm we store exact token counts (we do) and decide who owns updating lib/pricing.ts and whether cost is ever shown to users as 'estimated'.
- Minor (flagged per locked decision 2): OTP delivery via the Meta Cloud platform sender is auth infrastructure, not an agent channel — should its failures also be traced/counted in observability, or stay out of agent metrics? Default assumption: out of agent metrics.
- Should Test Console runs (OBS-17) count toward the per-tenant cost/budget that the Runtime/Gateway module enforces, or be tracked for visibility only?


### 4.5  Policy Engine, Human-in-the-Loop Approvals & Audit  `POL`  ·  21 tasks

This module adds the Phase 3 trust layer: one default-deny authorize(ctx,action) chokepoint over a per-action permission matrix (send_reply/bulk_send/delete/financial/booking/training_edit/org_admin x owner/member), central requireRole/requirePermission middleware that closes today's ungated routes (delete agent, edit training, org rename, lead CSV export), a generalized Approval queue + inbox that subsumes the existing approve-before-send draft while keeping /messages/:mid/approve\|skip working, and an append-only AuditLog wired into every privileged event. It establishes the non-negotiable rule that no model-emitted token ([[ALERT]], NEEDS_HUMAN, future tool_use) ever authorizes a privileged action, and adds a data-retention sweep. Everything is additive around the working reply loop, the alert/escalation path, and the existing draft approve/skip behavior, which stay byte-for-byte unchanged.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **POL-01** | Define the per-action permission matrix (default-deny) | BE | 3 | P0 | — | Create server/src/policy/permissions.ts: a typed catalog of action types (send_reply, bulk_send, delete, financial, booking, training_edit, org_admin) and permission levels (read_only \| auto \| with_approval \| prohibited), expressed as a matrix keyed by (role, action) and seeded default-deny so any unlisted pair resolves to deny. Exclude social_publish per the locked decisions (no LinkedIn/social). **Done:** Unit test proves an unlisted (role,action) returns deny; owner/member defaults match the documented matrix; the matrix is the single source consumed only via authorize(). |
| **POL-02** | authorize(ctx, action) policy engine chokepoint | BE | 3 | P0 | POL-01 | Create server/src/policy/engine.ts exporting a pure, synchronous authorize(ctx,{action,resource?}) -> 'allow' \| 'require_approval' \| 'deny' that resolves ctx.user.role against the POL-01 matrix with default-deny. No model/LLM involvement; with_approval maps to the Approval queue. **Done:** member+delete -> deny; owner+send_reply(auto) -> allow; owner+bulk_send(with_approval) -> require_approval; unknown action -> deny; branch-covered by unit tests. |
| **POL-03** | authz middleware: requireRole / requirePermission | Security | 3 | P0 | POL-02 | Create server/src/middleware/authz.ts with requireRole('owner') and requirePermission(action) that call authorize() and return 403 {error,code:'forbidden'} on deny, attaching the decision to req. Reuse the existing {error,code} envelope and req.user from auth.ts. **Done:** A member hitting a requirePermission('delete') route gets 403 forbidden; owner passes; already-allowed routes are behavior-unchanged. |
| **POL-04** | Gate the currently-ungated mutating routes | Security | 3 | P0 | POL-03 | Apply requirePermission/requireRole to the routes any member can hit today: delete agent (agents router), edit training (PUT /training/profile, sources create/patch/delete, apply-template), org rename (PATCH /api/org in routes.ts), lead CSV export (GET /api/leads/export). Replace the 5 inline owner checks in team/billing with the central middleware so there is one authz path. **Done:** member -> 403 on delete-agent, edit-training, org-rename, lead-export; owner -> 200; existing team/billing owner-only behavior unchanged; a test asserts every mutating route carries an authz gate. |

- Token hardening (User.token -> tokenHash + tokenExpiresAt + rotate-on-login) and POST /api/auth/logout are listed in roadmap Phase 3 but belong to the sibling Identity & Secrets module; confirm that split so POL-14 (audit of auth) and the secret-access audit touchpoint have their endpoints/stores to hook. This is the minor Cloud-API/auth-infra open question the task flagged.
- Reply drafts in the Approvals inbox: union existing Message rows with status='draft' into the list (no engine change, recommended v1) vs. materialize a real Approval row per draft. Union keeps the hot reply loop byte-for-byte.
- Data-retention values: AuditLog retention window and default Approval expiresAt TTL, plus any India-specific retention/compliance constraints, need owner-set numbers.
- secret-access audit depends on the Identity module's encrypted ConnectedAccount/Credential store shipping; audit it now as a stub or gate behind that module landing?


### 4.6  Identity, Secrets & RBAC hardening  `SEC`  ·  21 tasks

This module hardens the authentication/authorization and secrets layer around the working phone+OTP login without changing the login UX: it replaces plaintext never-expiring bearer tokens (User.token) with hashed, expiring, rotate-on-login tokens plus a real logout; introduces app-level AES-256-GCM encryption (lib/crypto.ts + lib/secrets.ts with a keyId for rotation) and an encrypted ConnectedAccount/Credential store, migrating the plaintext Telegram botToken out of Agent.channelConfig; and adds a central default-deny RBAC chokepoint that gates the ~25 currently-ungated mutating routes. It also delivers the user-facing Connected Accounts / security settings screen and clean client session-expiry handling, and explicitly defers OAuth2 per-connector until a non-WhatsApp connector actually ships. Everything is additive and keeps phone+OTP + the reply loop byte-for-byte unchanged.

Keys come from env (AGENT_SECRET_KEY, plus an optional AGENT_SECRET_KEYS keyId->key map for rotation). This is the single shared primitive for secrets at rest. **Done:** Unit test: decrypt(encrypt(x))===x; the stored blob embeds its keyId; a tampered blob or wrong key throws rather than returning garbage; no key material appears in the blob prefix. |

**Open questions —**
- OTP delivery today uses the Meta WhatsApp Cloud API as a PLATFORM-LEVEL sender for signup/reset (lib/otp.ts -> lib/whatsappCloud.ts). Per locked decisions this is auth infrastructure, not an agent channel, and stays as-is; flag only: if we ever move OTP off Cloud API that is a separate minor decision, out of scope here.
- RBAC policy per action needs product sign-off: proposed owner-only = delete agent, PATCH /api/org, lead CSV export, billing, team, connected-account disconnect; member-allowed = training edits + agent connect/pause. Confirm the member-vs-owner split before wiring the authz table.
- Token model: single active bearer per user (rotate-on-login logs out other devices) vs a multi-session Session table (per-device logout). Default proposed: single active token now, Session table deferred — confirm acceptable for tier-2 multi-device users.
- Token expiry policy: absolute 30-day vs sliding/refresh-on-activity. Proposed sliding refresh so tier-2 users are not surprised by sudden logouts — confirm.
- Mass-logout window: the token-hash change (SEC-3) invalidates all existing plaintext tokens once. Confirm a single forced re-login on deploy is acceptable vs a dual-read compatibility window.


### 4.7  Agent Specification, Versioning & Lifecycle  `SPEC`  ·  28 tasks

This module gives every agent a single canonical, versioned definition without changing the working reply loop. It adds a zod AgentSpec that serializes the existing Agent + TrainingProfile + TrainingSource rows (with forward-declared empty tools/capabilities/triggers/workflowRefs so the shape is future-proof), an AgentVersion snapshot table with atomic publish/rollback, a 4-state lifecycle on a NEW Agent.lifecycleStatus column (never reusing Agent.status, which means connection), and the owner-facing version-history + revert + lifecycle UI. Live-edit stays the byte-for-byte default: currentVersionId=null means replies read live training exactly as today; publishing a snapshot is explicit and optional, and rollback is the highest-value payoff since the owner currently edits a live bot against prod with no undo. composeSystemPrompt remains the final compile target and loadTraining is the one integration point that chooses snapshot-vs-live.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SPEC-1** | Schema + migration: AgentVersion table, Agent.currentVersionId, Agent.lifecycleStatus | DB | 4 | P1 | Phase 1 (migration discipline) | Add an AgentVersion snapshot model (id, agentId, version Int, specJson String, status String @default("published"), note String?, createdByUserId String?, createdAt, @@unique([agentId, version]), @@index([agentId])) plus nullable Agent.currentVersionId (null = live-edit default) and Agent.lifecycleStatus String @default("Draft") on a NEW column that never touches Agent.status. Ship as a real Prisma migration, not a db push. **Done:** prisma migrate deploy creates AgentVersion + the two Agent columns additively; existing agents load with currentVersionId=null and lifecycleStatus='Draft'/'Active'; no existing column is altered and Agent.status is untouched. |

Used by every write path (apply-spec, publish, clone). **Done:** A spec with an unknown approval value or an extra fabricated field is rejected or stripped; a well-formed spec returns {ok:true, spec}; errors are returned as {error, code} shaped messages consistent with existing routes. |

| **SPEC-5** | applySpec(agentId, spec) — compile a spec back to rows | BE | 4 | P1 | SPEC-2, SPEC-3 | Add applySpec(agentId, spec) that validates then writes the spec into the real rows in one transaction: upsert TrainingProfile (persona), replace TrainingSource rows (knowledge), and update Agent scalar fields (name, channelConfig, autoSend from behavior.approval, hours/timezone/awayMessage, alertNumber/alertCondition). composeSystemPrompt in training.ts stays the final compile target and is NOT modified. | **SPEC-7** | Spec forward-compat shim (migrateSpec) | BE | 4 | P2 | SPEC-2 | Add migrateSpec(rawSpec) that upgrades an older specVersion snapshot to the current shape before applySpec/loadTraining use it, so historical AgentVersion.specJson rows stay restorable after the spec evolves. specVersion starts at 1; the shim is an identity function today with an explicit upgrade seam. **Done:** A snapshot with specVersion<current is upgraded (or clearly rejected with a friendly error) rather than crashing rollback/loadTraining; a specVersion=1 snapshot round-trips unchanged. |
| **SPEC-8** | loadTraining chooses snapshot when currentVersionId is set, else live | BE | 4 | P1 | SPEC-1, SPEC-4, SPEC-7 | Modify engine.ts loadTraining(agentId) to return the AgentVersion snapshot's profile+sources (decoded from specJson via migrateSpec) when agent.currentVersionId is set, otherwise the live TrainingProfile/TrainingSource exactly as today. This single change powers versioned replies for both processInbound and draftFor (and, transitively, the public widget). **Done:** With currentVersionId=null, loadTraining output and the resulting prompt are byte-identical to today; with currentVersionId set, composeSystemPrompt is built from the pinned snapshot's profile+sources; loadTraining takes agent (not just agentId) or reads currentVersionId internally without an extra hot-path round-trip surprise. |

A malformed row logs and is skipped or falls back to live, never throwing into the reply path. **Done:** Injecting a corrupt dataJson / specJson row produces a logged warning and a successful (degraded) reply or a safe live fallback; no 500 and no unhandled exception in processInbound or the training endpoints. |

Pure and unit-testable like training.ts helpers. **Done:** canTransition returns true only for allowed edges and false for illegal ones (e.g. Archived→Active without an explicit restore); a unit test covers every edge; the module imports nothing that reads Agent.status. |

Add an Archived guard in processInbound so an archived agent logs inbound but does not reply (alongside the existing paused/takenOver/muted check). **Done:** Creating an agent yields Draft; going live yields Active; pausing yields Paused and resuming Active; an Archived agent records the inbound message but returns status 'ignored' and never sends. |

Tenant-scoped via requireAgent. **Done:** Returns an ordered list with an isCurrent flag matching Agent.currentVersionId; another org's agent id returns 404 via requireAgent. |
| **SPEC-15** | Rollback endpoint: POST /api/agents/:id/rollback | API | 4 | P1 | SPEC-5, SPEC-13, SPEC-7 | Restore a prior version atomically: point currentVersionId at the chosen AgentVersion AND applySpec its snapshot back onto the live TrainingProfile/TrainingSource/Agent rows so the Train UI reflects the restored config and the owner can keep editing. This is the highest-value payoff (the owner edits a live bot with no undo today). **Done:** Rollback to version N restores that snapshot's prompt-affecting rows in one transaction and the next reply uses it; rolling back to a non-existent version returns a friendly 404; the operation is idempotent. |

Enables one-tap duplication and 'save this agent as my starting point'. **Done:** Clone creates a new Draft agent with identical training but its own id/session, respects the maxAgents plan gate (402 when exceeded), and does not copy the source's channel connection or conversations. |

Reuse the SetupGuide confirm/invisible-UI patterns. **Done:** Owner can open history, see versions with current marked, tap Revert on an older one, and see a confirming 'show-what-changed' result; the list refreshes and the header reflects the active version. Copy must reinforce that editing applies to the next message with no retrain ('Saved — your agent already knows this'), while Publish is a deliberate, named checkpoint ('Publish v3'). No forced publish anywhere; zero snapshots exist until the owner chooses to publish. **Done:** Publishing is a single explicit action that creates a labeled version; nothing auto-publishes; the Train UI still saves live edits instantly without a retrain step; the copy distinguishes live-save from publish. |
| **SPEC-23** | Archive / Restore + Duplicate controls in the agent header/menu | UX | 4 | P2 | SPEC-12, SPEC-18, SPEC-20 | Add outcome-named Archive (stops replies, moves to Archived) and Restore (back to Active/Paused) and a Duplicate action (calls clone) in the AgentDetail header overflow. Archiving shows its side effect ('Archived — it will no longer reply'); Duplicate navigates to the new Draft agent. **Done:** Archive/Restore flip lifecycleStatus and show the consequence inline; an archived agent visibly stops replying; Duplicate creates and opens a Draft copy; all buttons are outcome-named, no Save/Submit. |
| **SPEC-24** | API/integration tests for versioning, rollback, lifecycle & clone | Test | 4 | P1 | SPEC-13, SPEC-14, SPEC-15, SPEC-17, SPEC-18, SPEC-19 | Cover the HTTP surface in the Vitest api suite: publish creates a numbered version and sets currentVersionId; list returns isCurrent correctly; rollback restores snapshot rows and is tenant-isolated (404 cross-org); apply-spec rejects invalid specs with no write; clone respects the plan gate; a malformed AgentVersion.specJson does not 500; member-role is 403 on mutations. **Done:** All listed cases pass; cross-tenant access to another org's versions/rollback returns 404; invalid apply-spec leaves training unchanged; the suite runs green in CI. |
| **SPEC-25** | Thread active version into the execution trace (analytics by version) | BE | 4 | P2 | SPEC-8, Phase 2 (execution trace / UsageEvent extension) | Stamp the agent's effective version (currentVersionId or 'live') onto the execution-trace row written per reply, so success/intervention/cost metrics can be grouped by version and a bad publish's impact is measurable and reversible with evidence. Additive to the Phase 2 trace/UsageEvent extension. **Done:** Each reply's trace row records the version used (or 'live'); analytics can aggregate metrics per version; a rollback is visible in the trace stream. |
| **SPEC-26** | Document the Agent Spec, versioning & lifecycle model | Docs | 4 | P2 | SPEC-1, SPEC-2, SPEC-13, SPEC-15 | Reconcile the drifted docs to describe what now exists: add AgentVersion, Agent.currentVersionId and Agent.lifecycleStatus to DATA_MODEL.md; document the zod AgentSpec shape, the round-trip contract, the live-vs-snapshot rule (currentVersionId=null = live default), and the 4-state lifecycle vs the connection Agent.status distinction; note publish/rollback/clone endpoints. **Done:** Docs accurately describe the new tables/columns, the spec shape, the lifecycle states (and that they are separate from connection status), and the publish/rollback/clone APIs; no claim that outstrips the code. |
| ✚ **SPEC-27** | Redact channel secrets from AgentSpec serialization, version snapshots and GET /spec | Security | 4 | P1 | SPEC-4, SPEC-13, SPEC-16, SEC-2, SEC-9 | specFromAgent serializes Agent.channelConfig into the spec, which today stores the Telegram botToken in plaintext JSON. Publish (SPEC-13) persists that spec into AgentVersion.specJson and GET /api/agents/:id/spec (SPEC-16) plus the NL-build response return it, so a channel credential would be snapshotted at rest and leaked to the client. Make the spec's channelConfig carry only non-secret config; redact/strip secret keys (botToken, future OAuth tokens) on specFromAgent and reference the ConnectedAccount/secrets store; re-resolve the live secret (not the snapshot copy) on applySpec/rollback so a snapshot never carries or reinstates a stale credential. **Done:** specFromAgent/GET /api/agents/:id/spec never emit botToken or any secret key; a published AgentVersion.specJson contains no credential; rollback/applySpec restores channel settings without overwriting or exposing the live secret; a test asserts no secret string appears in a serialized spec, a version snapshot, or the spec API response. |
| ✚ **SPEC-28** | Backfill Agent.lifecycleStatus for existing agents from current connection/paused state | DB | 4 | P2 | SPEC-1, SPEC-11 | SPEC-1 adds Agent.lifecycleStatus with @default('Draft'), so the column-add makes EVERY existing agent 'Draft' — including live, connected, replying bots — mislabeling the lifecycle chip (SPEC-20) for the whole installed base. Add a one-time data backfill (same pattern as DB-7..10 orgId backfills) deriving lifecycleStatus from current state: connected ChannelSession and not paused -> Active; paused -> Paused; never-connected/draft -> Draft, honoring canTransition edges and never touching Agent.status (connection). **Done:** After the migration an existing connected non-paused agent reads lifecycleStatus='Active', a paused agent 'Paused', an unconnected/draft agent 'Draft'; Agent.status is unchanged; the backfill is idempotent and ships as a real Prisma migration (no db push); a test on a seeded mix of agents verifies the mapping. |

**UX coverage —** Agent detail header (AgentDetail.tsx hero): a lifecycle badge (Draft/Active/Paused/Archived) shown distinctly from the connection StatusPill; Version history panel with per-version note/date/current marker and a 'Revert to this version' control (SPEC-21), reusing SetupGuide's invisible-UI confirm patterns; Explicit optional 'Publish this version' control with a note field, worded so live-edit remains the instant default and publish is a deliberate checkpoint (SPEC-22); Archive/Restore and Duplicate (clone) actions in the agent header with show-what-changed feedback (SPEC-23); Show-what-changed messaging after revert/publish/archive instead of silent toasts, per UX_PRINCIPLES; Training panel copy that keeps 'Saved — your agent already knows this' instant-edit promise alongside optional publish.

**Open questions —**
- Should a published version/snapshot also pin operational toggles (autoSend, business hours, alertNumber/condition), or should those always read live while only persona+knowledge are versioned? (Proposed: version persona+knowledge only; operational toggles stay live.)
- On rollback, overwrite the live TrainingProfile/TrainingSource rows from the snapshot (so Train UI reflects it and editing resumes) in addition to swapping currentVersionId? (Proposed: yes — restore rows + keep pointer; owner can clear the pin to return to pure live-edit.)
- Should lifecycleStatus 'Paused' be the single source of truth or stay mirrored to the existing Agent.paused boolean to avoid dual state? (Proposed: mirror Agent.paused to avoid drift.)
- Does Archived hard-stop replies only, or also stop/disconnect the whatsapp-web.js ChannelSession to free the Chromium instance? (Proposed: stop replies now; optionally release the session later for resource savings.)
- Keep publishing fully manual (zero snapshots until the owner clicks Publish), or auto-create a first version on 'go live' so rollback always has a baseline? (Proposed: manual, to protect the live-edit default — revisit if owners want an automatic safety point.)


### 4.8  Agent Creation Experience / Studio  `STU`  ·  54 tasks

The Studio is the product's centerpiece: anyone describes a personal agent in plain words (or pastes a link / picks a role template), reviews a validated AgentSpec DRAFT (never code), simulates it, and goes live in a guided zero-decision flow. Every path — natural-language, template, and form — compiles through one applySpec() write-path so NL and visual always produce the same definition, with versioning + rollback and clone on top, built additively around the existing learn.ts/templates.ts/SetupGuide/training model and the single composeSystemPrompt compile target. It kills the hardcoded client channel drift by generating pickers from GET /api/channels, keeps the reply loop and instant-edit promise byte-for-byte, and defers the visual node-graph builder until the spec + workflow spine exist.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **STU-1** | Define the zod AgentSpec schema (lib/agentSpec.ts) | BE | 4 | P1 | — | Create a versioned zod AgentSpec that is the canonical serialization of existing rows: identity, channel{type,channelConfig}, persona{instructions,restrictions,tone,language,escalationKeyword}, knowledge[{type:'fact'\|'faq'\|'link',data}], behavior{approval,hours,timezone,awayMessage,alerts{number,condition}}, plus forward-declared empty arrays tools/capabilities/workflowRefs/evaluationCriteria. It introduces no new executable concepts and is a strict superset of Agent + TrainingProfile + TrainingSource. **Done:** validateSpec() accepts a spec covering every UI-settable field, defaults forward-declared arrays to [], and rejects unknown/extra fields and malformed knowledge entries. |
| **STU-2** | specFromAgent(agentId) serializer | BE | 4 | P1 | STU-1 | Pure function that loads an agent's Agent row + TrainingProfile + TrainingSource rows and emits a valid AgentSpec. Mirrors the shapes already parsed in the agents router loadSources() and training.ts. **Done:** For any existing agent, specFromAgent returns a spec that validateSpec() accepts and that contains every fact/FAQ/link and persona/behavior field currently stored. |

BOTH the NL builder and the form/template path MUST route through this so NL and visual produce identical definitions. **Done:** Calling applySpec writes rows such that the agent replies exactly as the equivalent form edits would; composeSystemPrompt output is unchanged for an unchanged spec; no path writes training rows except through applySpec. |

The POL-01 matrix has 8 action types and none is create_agent, while POST /api/agents has no role check today; once default-deny lands, creation would be denied-by-default. Per the north star, members are permitted to create/build/clone; reconcile STU-7's 'owner-authenticated' wording. **Done:** A member can describe->create and clone an agent; the default-deny authz layer explicitly permits create_agent/build/apply-spec/clone/upload for both member and owner; the ungated-route guard meta-test (QA-22/SEC-12) includes these creation routes; no creation route bypasses authorize(). |

Show channel-specific inputs (location/images/quick-replies, and the WhatsApp-only owner-alert number) only when the selected channel supports them, replacing hardcoded isWhatsapp checks in SetupGuide.tsx. Overlaps PXD-9 (data-driven capability catalog + no-hardcode guard) and must be reconciled so one task owns consuming channel capability flags vs the agent-ability catalog. **Done:** Selecting a channel shows only the inputs its capabilities support; a website/telegram agent no longer sees WhatsApp-only alert fields via a hardcoded isWhatsapp check but via capability flags; adding a capability to a registered adapter surfaces its field in the builder with no other client change. |

**UX coverage —** Agent gallery/home (Your agents) — distinct agents by role+channel+live status, with Create-by-describe and Template-gallery entry points; Empty state — 'Create your first agent' dispatch leading with describe-in-words; Create Agent (describe-in-words) screen at /agents/new — one textarea + optional link paste; Spec-draft review/edit screen — plain-English draft (name, how-it-replies, facts/FAQs/links chips, channel, approval) before Create; Role/vertical template gallery — one-tap tiles that pre-fill the builder draft; Channel picker step — generated from /api/channels, default pre-selected, not-ready channels disabled; Capability/tool picker ('what can this agent DO'); Trigger picker ('when does it act': on message / on schedule / on webhook); Approval/permission picker ('what needs my OK'); Simulate/Test-before-go-live step (reuse Ask-your-agent-anything); Generalized guided zero-decision setup wizard (channel-agnostic SetupGuide); Publish + version-history screen with show-what-changed and live-edit indicator; Rollback (restore a version) flow; Clone/duplicate agent action; File/document upload ingestion in builder + TrainingPanel; Builder loading/thinking/empty/error/model-refusal/warnings states; i18n (en/hi/te), 44px tap targets, safe-area, phone-width layouts across all Studio screens.

**Open questions —**
- Does describe-in-words REPLACE the current channel-first picker in Agents.tsx as the default entry, or sit alongside it as a second path? (recommend lead with describe, keep channel-first as a fallback)
- Capability/tool picker and schedule/webhook triggers can only be wired to real behavior once the Tools (Phase 6) and Event/Workflow (Phase 5) modules land — in Phase 4 these render as forward-declared/'soon' choices. Confirm that staged exposure is acceptable rather than hiding them until then.
- Scope of file/doc upload at Phase 4: plain text + basic PDF extraction into a training draft now, with chunking/pgvector deferred to the Knowledge/RAG module (Phase 6). Confirm no large-catalog ingestion is expected pre-RAG.
- OTP delivery still uses Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender (auth infra, not an agent channel). The channel picker/registry must never surface a Cloud-API 'agent' channel — flagging as the noted minor open question; no change planned.
- Should role templates that imply tools (appointment-booking, website-monitor) be creatable in Phase 4 as persona-only agents (capability greyed 'coming soon'), or hidden until their tool/workflow handler ships?


### 4.9  Event, Trigger & Workflow Spine (Postgres-backed)  `EWS`  ·  34 tasks

This module adds the additive, Postgres-backed spine that turns the single-turn reply bot into an agent platform able to run scheduled, webhook-driven, and multi-step proactive work: a CloudEvents-shaped DomainEvent outbox, Trigger/Job/WorkflowRun/WorkflowStepLog tables, a SKIP-LOCKED lease-claim runner with backoff/DLQ/replay, a node-cron ticker, workflow/trigger registries, a raw-body webhooks router that finally makes Razorpay billing server-authoritative, and an owner-facing Automations/Runs view. Everything is gated behind WORKFLOW_ENABLED (default off => byte-for-byte today) and keeps the hot reply loop inline, so the proven processInbound path, channel-adapter seam, and training model never change. The spine only hosts handlers (outbound-drip, scheduled-monitor, approval-gated-send) that other modules register; it owns the generic infrastructure, state machine, isolation, observability hooks, UX, tests, and the pg-boss graduation decision.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **EWS-01** | DomainEvent outbox table (CloudEvents field-shape) | DB | 5 | P1 | Migrations | Add a Postgres-backed DomainEvent/outbox model: id, orgId, source, type, time, subject?, dataJson, idempotencyKey, processedAt?, createdAt. It is the transactional outbox a dispatcher drains to match triggers; it is NOT a broker. **Done:** Migration applies via `prisma migrate deploy` (never db push); a DomainEvent row is written in the same transaction as the domain change; rows are filterable by orgId and by processedAt via an index. |
| **EWS-02** | Trigger table (event + cron + webhook kinds) | DB | 5 | P1 | EWS-01, Migrations | Add a Trigger model: id, orgId, agentId?, kind('event'\|'cron'\|'webhook'), eventType?, cronExpr?, filterJson, handlerName, enabled, nextRunAt?, createdAt. One row binds a source signal to a registered workflow handler. **Done:** Can persist an event trigger and a cron trigger for the same agent; a disabled trigger is never matched/fired; indexes on (enabled, kind) and (eventType) serve the dispatcher. |
| **EWS-03** | Job queue table (status, lease, idempotencyKey @unique) | DB | 5 | P1 | EWS-01, Migrations | Add the Job model that is the runner's claimable unit: id, orgId, workflowRunId?, handlerName, status(pending\|leased\|running\|completed\|failed\|dead), nextRunAt, attempts, maxAttempts, stateJson, lease, leaseUntil, idempotencyKey @unique, lastError, createdAt, updatedAt. **Done:** A duplicate enqueue with the same idempotencyKey is rejected by the unique constraint; the claim query is covered by a (status, nextRunAt) index and a (leaseUntil) index for reclaim. |
| **EWS-04** | WorkflowRun + WorkflowStepLog tables | DB | 5 | P1 | EWS-01, Migrations | Add WorkflowRun (id, orgId, agentId?, workflowName, status(running\|sleeping\|awaiting_approval\|paused\|completed\|failed\|canceled), currentStep, stateJson, startedAt, updatedAt, finishedAt?) and append-only WorkflowStepLog (id, runId, stepName, result, detailJson, createdAt) for durable history/replay. **Done:** A run's full history is reconstructable from WorkflowStepLog rows; every status transition is persisted; both carry orgId for isolation. |
| **EWS-05** | events/envelope.ts — CloudEvents field-shape type + makeEvent() | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-01 | Define a TS type and a pure makeEvent() for the envelope (id, source, type, time, subject?, data) — field-shape only, no CloudEvents SDK and no broker, per the locked lightest-sufficient stance. **Done:** Unit test asserts the envelope shape and deterministic id/time; no new npm dependency is added (grep package.json). |
| **EWS-06** | emit() outbox writer + instrument key domain events | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-01, EWS-05 | Add events/emit.ts that persists a DomainEvent (outbox) fire-and-forget, mirroring alertOwner's try/catch so it never throws into the hot path. Instrument a small set of signals (message.received, lead.created, billing.payment) without altering reply behavior. **Done:** Emitting from processInbound writes a DomainEvent row but leaves reply output/latency and existing tests unchanged; with WORKFLOW_ENABLED off, no downstream dispatch happens. |
| **EWS-07** | workflow/types.ts — WorkflowHandler + StepResult union | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-04 | Define StepResult = complete \| retry{afterMs} \| sleep{until} \| awaitApproval{approvalRef} \| fail{error}, plus WorkflowHandler and a StepContext ({orgId, agentId, run, state, emit, log}). This is the tiny contract the runner drives. **Done:** Typecheck passes; a reference handler implements WorkflowHandler and returns each StepResult variant; the union is exhaustively switched in the runner (no default-fallthrough). |
| **EWS-08** | workflow/registry.ts — handler registry (mirrors channel registry) | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-07 | In-process Map registry with registerWorkflow/getWorkflow/listWorkflows, self-registering at module load exactly like channels/registry.ts. Other modules (outbound-drip, scheduled-monitor, approval-gated-send) register their handlers here; the spine owns no business handlers. **Done:** A handler module self-registers on import; getWorkflow(unknown) returns undefined and the runner dead-letters the job instead of crashing; listWorkflows() enumerates registered handlers. |
| **EWS-09** | triggers/registry.ts + event→trigger dispatcher (outbox drain) | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-02, EWS-03, EWS-06, EWS-08 | triggers/registry.ts plus a dispatcher that reads unprocessed DomainEvents, matches enabled Triggers by type/filter, enqueues a Job (or opens a WorkflowRun) with a derived idempotencyKey, then marks the event processedAt — all in one transaction. **Done:** An event with one matching trigger enqueues exactly one job (re-draining the same event enqueues nothing due to idempotencyKey); an event with no match is marked processed with no job. |
| **EWS-10** | jobs/runner.ts — SKIP LOCKED lease-claim loop, backoff, DLQ | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-03, EWS-07, EWS-08 | The core runner: claim due jobs via SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED, set lease/leaseUntil, dispatch to the handler, apply the returned StepResult, exponential backoff on retry (updates nextRunAt/attempts), dead-letter at attempts>=maxAttempts, append a WorkflowStepLog row, renew/release the lease. **Done:** Two concurrent claimers never execute the same job (lease proves single ownership); a repeatedly-failing step retries with growing backoff and becomes status='dead' at maxAttempts with lastError recorded. |
| **EWS-11** | Dialect-aware claim (SQLite dev / Postgres prod parity) | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-10 | SKIP LOCKED is Postgres-only but the dev datasource is SQLite; provide a single claim abstraction that uses FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED on Postgres and a serialized transactional UPDATE...WHERE id IN (SELECT ... LIMIT n) on SQLite, so the runner and the whole test suite run locally. **Done:** The runner claims and completes jobs on both SQLite (dev/test) and Postgres; a single-claim test passes under both providers. |
| **EWS-12** | WorkflowRun stepping + StepResult transitions | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-04, EWS-10 | Advance a WorkflowRun across ticks: sleep -> set nextRunAt; retry -> reschedule same step; awaitApproval -> park run (status=awaiting_approval, enqueue nothing); complete -> next step or finish; fail -> dead-letter. Each step appends WorkflowStepLog and updates stateJson/currentStep. **Done:** A two-step reference run advances over successive ticks; a sleep step resumes exactly at nextRunAt; run status mirrors each transition and is queryable. |
| **EWS-13** | scheduler.ts — node-cron ~30–60s ticker for cron/schedule triggers | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-02, EWS-03, EWS-09 | A single node-cron ticker that evaluates due cron/schedule Triggers and enqueues their jobs (generalizing the ALERT_COOLDOWN_MS/alertedAt persisted-timer idiom). It does not run handlers itself; it only enqueues, the runner executes. **Done:** A cron trigger due 'now' is enqueued within one tick (<=60s); a disabled or not-yet-due trigger enqueues nothing; no duplicate enqueue across two overlapping ticks. |

Add a double-start guard and SIGTERM handling that stops the ticker and releases held leases so a redeploy never strands jobs. **Done:** With the flag unset, neither ticker nor runner starts and the process behaves byte-for-byte as today; on SIGTERM, leases held by this process are released (leaseUntil cleared) and the ticker stops. |

| **EWS-17** | POST /api/webhooks/razorpay — server-authoritative billing | Security | 5 | P1 | EWS-16, EWS-09 | Wire the already-written verifyWebhookSignature: verify HMAC, dedupe on provider event id (emit a DomainEvent idempotently), and apply the plan server-side so a paid checkout upgrades the org even if the browser never calls /verify. Explicitly NO Meta inbound webhook (Cloud API for agents is OUT); OTP cloud sender stays as-is. **Done:** Invalid signature -> 401; duplicate event id -> ignored; a valid payment.captured upgrades the subscription without any client /verify call; no Meta inbound route exists. |

Approve re-authorizes server-side and resumes a parked run; no model-emitted token ever authorizes a privileged resume. **Done:** Pause stops further steps; cancel is terminal; approve resumes an awaiting_approval run exactly once (second approve is a no-op); a member hits 403 where the permission table forbids it. |
| **EWS-20** | Tenant isolation + untrusted-event-data posture across the spine | Security | 5 | P1 | EWS-03, EWS-10, EWS-18 | Guarantee every Trigger/Job/Run/DomainEvent carries orgId and that the runner and all APIs filter by it; cap and treat event.data/stateJson as opaque data (never concatenated into a system prompt or executed), closing the prompt-injection and cross-tenant surfaces for proactive runs. **Done:** A job for org A cannot act on org B's agent (isolation test); oversized or malicious event data is rejected/truncated and logged, not run; no spine field flows unescaped into composeSystemPrompt. |

| **EWS-22** | Spine metrics + pino structured logs | BE | 5 | P2 | EWS-10, Observability | Emit one redacted structured log line per claim/step and expose SQL-queryable metrics: queue depth, due backlog, DLQ count, run outcomes, step latency. Feeds the Phase 2 execution-trace/analytics rather than a separate store. **Done:** Queue depth / DLQ count / outcome breakdowns are returned by SQL aggregation; each job execution emits exactly one pino line with no secret/PII. |

This generalizes approve-before-send to any gated workflow step (e.g. approve-before-book, approve-before-drip). **Done:** An approval-gated run sends nothing until approved, then resumes from the parked step exactly once; a rejected or expired approval never executes the gated step. |

Update DATA_MODEL.md with the new tables and reconcile the drifted Redis/worker claims. **Done:** A developer can register a new workflow handler + trigger by following the guide without editing runner.ts; DATA_MODEL.md lists DomainEvent/Trigger/Job/WorkflowRun/WorkflowStepLog accurately. |

Explicitly keep Temporal/Kafka/A2A out per the locked stance; nothing is built now. **Done:** A written decision record names the exact flip metric (e.g. sustained queue depth / throughput) and states that until it fires the runner stays as-is; no pg-boss/Redis dependency is added. |
| ✚ **EWS-32** | Inbound-path durability / backpressure — durable dispatch or recorded keep-inline decision | BE | 5 | P1 | EWS-03, EWS-10, FND-30, FND-31, MGW-2 | MERGED (Phase-5 EWS + cross-cut §11/§13): target-arch §13 requires a Postgres inbound-dispatch queue (concurrency cap + retry/backoff + dead-letter) replacing today's 'a failed Claude call silently drops the message', but target-arch §5 + the roadmap keep the hot reply loop strictly inline — a direct §13-vs-§5 contradiction that leaves QA-49 orphaned. FND-30 only adds an in-memory lock and MGW-2 only an in-request retry, so a crash/exhausted-retry mid-model-call still loses the inbound message. Either make inbound durable by reusing the Job/runner spine (hot path stays inline when WORKFLOW_ENABLED off) OR record an ADR fixing inbound-stays-synchronous with its named accepted data-loss window + bounded in-process mitigation. **Done:** A written decision-log ADR reconciles §13 (queue replaces drops) with §5 (reply loop inline); under an inbound burst no message is silently lost — a crashed/failed dispatch is persisted and retried with backoff then dead-lettered (or, for keep-inline, the residual drop-risk + bounded retry/concurrency mitigation are documented and tested); QA-49 is re-pointed to depend on THIS task and passes; WORKFLOW_ENABLED=off / executionMode='reply' stays byte-for-byte and the FND-30 lock is preserved. |
| ✚ **EWS-33** | Retention/TTL prune sweep for DomainEvent outbox, terminal Jobs & WorkflowStepLog | Infra | 5 | P2 | EWS-01, EWS-03, EWS-04, EWS-34 | The spine's highest-volume additions grow unbounded — processed DomainEvent outbox rows, completed/dead Job rows, and the append-only WorkflowStepLog history. OBS-18 prunes traces+Message, POL-17 prunes approvals/audit, MK-12 prunes ContactMemory, but no task prunes the spine tables. Add an idempotent, configurable-window prune sweep that registers on the shared maintenance ticker (new EWS-34) rather than standing up its own scheduler. **Done:** Processed DomainEvent rows, terminal (completed/dead) Jobs, and WorkflowStepLog rows past the configured window are deleted on the next sweep; the sweep is idempotent and leaves in-flight runs / unprocessed outbox events intact; it runs on the EWS-34 ticker (one ticker, not a new setInterval); the default retention window is documented in DATA_MODEL.md. |
| ✚ **EWS-34** | Shared always-on maintenance scheduler seam (ungated by WORKFLOW_ENABLED) | Infra | 2 | P2 | — | A tiny in-process node-cron scheduler started at boot INDEPENDENT of WORKFLOW_ENABLED, exposing registerMaintenanceSweep(name, cronExpr, fn) so every retention/TTL sweep shares ONE ticker. Deliberately distinct from EWS-13 (the trigger-evaluation ticker, which stays WORKFLOW_ENABLED-gated and Phase 5): housekeeping must run even on default deployments where the flag is off, and the first consumer (OBS-18) lands in Phase 2, before the spine exists. Resolves the §5.4 note that assumed all sweeps reuse the Phase-5 flag-gated ticker — they cannot (it is both later and off-by-default). OBS-18, POL-17, MK-12 and EWS-33 register through this seam. **Done:** One node-cron ticker starts at boot regardless of WORKFLOW_ENABLED and runs registered sweeps on their cron; adding a sweep needs no scheduler edit; with no sweeps registered the process behaves byte-for-byte as today; a double-start guard and SIGTERM clean-stop exist; OBS-18/POL-17/MK-12/EWS-33 each register their sweep here instead of creating an independent interval. |

**UX coverage —** New Automations/Runs page (route /automations) + nav item in AppShell listing every workflow run; Outcome-named run statuses (Running / Waiting for your OK / Paused / Done / Failed) instead of raw enums; Run detail view with a plain-language step timeline reconstructed from WorkflowStepLog; Pause / Resume / Cancel / Approve controls as outcome-named buttons with show-what-changed confirmation (not just a toast); Empty-state dispatch (No automations yet) rather than a blank list; Live run-status updates via the new 'workflow' WS event (no reload); Filters/history (by agent, status, date) for completed/failed runs; Zero-decision defaults and <=3 controls before the primary action per UX_PRINCIPLES.md.

**Open questions —**
- OTP delivery still uses the Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender (signup/reset) via whatsappCloud.ts/otp.ts; locked decisions keep this as auth infrastructure, not an agent channel. Confirm we leave it untouched and do NOT add a Meta INBOUND webhook to the new webhooks router (Cloud API for agents is OUT).
- Webhook dedupe store: reuse DomainEvent.idempotencyKey for provider message-id dedupe, or add a dedicated WebhookEvent table? Leaning on DomainEvent to avoid a table.
- DomainEvent/WorkflowStepLog retention: growth is unbounded; need a TTL/prune or archival policy (node-cron sweep) before volume grows.
- numReplicas stays 1 for now; the lease/leaseUntil columns are added anyway so a future second worker claims safely. Confirm single-ticker assumption (double-start guard) is acceptable until the measured pg-boss/worker-split trigger fires.
- Confirm the per-agent send mutex (owned by the Transport/Outbound module) is the coordination point the runner must acquire before any outbound-drip send, so drips never collide with live replies.


### 4.10  Standard Scenarios & Outbound (whatsapp-web ONLY)  `SCN`  ·  42 tasks

This module realizes the five locked tier-2 scenarios on the shared spine as config + tool + trigger, with the single-turn reply loop and channel-adapter registry left byte-for-byte intact: (1) harden the existing WhatsApp reply agent, (2) a Trackly-model whatsapp-web QR outbound drip (accepted-risk, secondary number, cap/window/jitter/warmup, STOP opt-out, send-ledger, per-agent send mutex) with the Cloud-API BROADCAST path explicitly excluded, (3) appointment booking (calendar tool + approve-before-book + double-book guard + Booking record), (4) website offer/price monitoring (Watch table + scheduled fetch/diff/notify via the post-SSRF-fix learn.ts), and (5) structural multi-agent (many agents per org + a shared lead/task record; in-process delegation deferred). It adds per-scenario Studio setup plus a campaign/drip console, a monitors console, and a booking view, all behind feature flags that default off so nothing that works today regresses. Everything here consumes the foundation/gateway/policy/spine/tools modules rather than re-building them.

Parameterize the channel display name from the adapter so the same reply engine serves every scenario channel identically. **Done:** A website and a telegram agent get a prompt that names their own channel (no 'WhatsApp' literal); WhatsApp reply output is byte-identical to today for the same input; a unit test asserts the channel label is injected from the adapter. |

Asserts the Security module's per-socket WS auth protects the reply path. **Done:** A socket without a valid token, or with a token for a different org, is rejected when subscribing to an agentId it does not own; no qr/message/alert frame is delivered cross-tenant. |

The drip handler must suppress any number present in OutboundOptOut before sending. **Done:** A 'STOP' reply suppresses the contact (OptOut row), reports opted_out, sends one confirmation, and triggers no AI reply; a subsequent drip tick skips that number; any other reply flows to the AI agent exactly as today. |

Records that BROADCAST (Cloud API) and flight/LinkedIn are out of scope. **Done:** A single matrix maps each of the five scenarios to its transport, ban-risk posture, and mitigations; it states BROADCAST/flight/LinkedIn are excluded and OTP Cloud-API is OTP-only. |

Add server/src/modules/outbound/router.ts with connection CRUD, a campaign-start endpoint (enqueues the bulk_send Approval + creates the WorkflowRun, sending nothing until approved), a ledger-aggregated progress read, and opt-out list/delete; pause/resume/cancel delegate to EWS-19 run control (campaign = WorkflowRun), not a second implementation. **Done:** An owner creates/lists/updates/deletes an OutboundConnection; its Trackly token is stored only via the encrypted secrets store (never logged) and a connection cannot activate without a persisted risk acknowledgement; POST campaign-start creates a with-approval Approval (POL-08) and sends nothing until approved; GET campaign returns live sent/delivered/read/failed/opted-out counts from OutboundSendLog; the owner can list/remove OutboundOptOut rows; all endpoints org-scoped (cross-org id -> 404) and authz-gated; pause/resume/cancel reuse EWS-19. |

Confirming a PENDING booking is the Approval queue approve (POL-08/POL-10), but a confirmed booking is post-approval, so SCN-24's 'cancel in one tap' and its upcoming/past list have nothing to call (the Approvals inbox lists only pending items). Add a bookings read + cancel/reschedule API reusing the SCN-22 double-book guard on reschedule and writing an AuditLog row on cancel. **Done:** An owner lists upcoming and past bookings for an agent, org-scoped (cross-org id -> 404); cancelling a confirmed booking sets status=cancelled and writes exactly one AuditLog row; rescheduling re-runs the SCN-22 double-book guard transactionally and rejects an overlapping confirmed slot; confirming a PENDING booking continues to route through the POL-08 Approval queue (not a duplicate confirm path). |
| ✚ **SCN-42** | Watches/Schedule CRUD API (materialize the phantom 'SCN-27b') | API | 5 | P1 | SCN-27, SCN-28, FND-15 | target-architecture §10 and roadmap Phase-5 both require POST/GET/DELETE /api/agents/:id/watches so an owner can create/list/delete website-monitor watches, but SCN-27 is the Watch/Schedule DATA MODEL only and the SCN + §9/§10 audits both cite the endpoint as covered by 'SCN-27b (completeness-critic addendum)' — a task that is ABSENT from the 479-task inventory. Materialize the watches CRUD endpoint so the monitors console/wizard (SCN-30) has a backend to call. If 'SCN-27b' already exists in a backlog addendum, reconcile this to it rather than duplicate. **Done:** An owner creates/lists/deletes a Watch for an agent (url, cron/interval, diff target), org-scoped (cross-org id -> 404) and authz-gated; a created Watch registers an on-schedule Trigger onto the EWS spine; the SCN-30 monitors console drives entirely off this API; the fetch URL is validated against the FND-15 SSRF allowlist on create. |

**UX coverage —** Studio 'What should this agent do?' per-scenario picker with zero-decision defaults and <=3 controls before the primary action; Outbound campaign/drip console: live progress (sent/delivered/read/failed/opted-out), one-tap pause/resume/stop, cap+window+warmup behind one named disclosure; Outbound campaign setup wizard: written accepted-risk consent, pick/secondary-number nudge, connect Trackly token, outcome-named 'Start sending' button; STOP opt-out confirmation message copy and an opt-out list view per agent; Monitors console + 'Watch a page for changes' wizard (paste link -> cadence -> notify me), showing last checked / last changed / current snapshot; Booking view: upcoming + past bookings, approve/confirm/cancel, double-book surfaced in-line; Reply-agent hardening (channel-aware prompt, WS-auth-protected realtime) with no visible UX change; Feature-gated nav entries: Campaigns, Monitors, Bookings (data-driven navigation.ts).

**Open questions —**
- Trackly auth style for the connector: OAuth2 client-credentials vs a signed Personal Access Token (plan recommends PAT for v1, migrate later).
- Which number sends the drip: the primary business line or a dedicated secondary number (strongly recommend secondary given accepted ban risk).
- Appointment-booking calendar backend near-term: internal Booking table only (no external dependency) vs Google Calendar OAuth2 (OAuth connectors are deferred to Phase 7 by the roadmap).
- Minor open question per locked decision #2: OTP currently uses Meta Cloud API as a platform-level signup/reset sender — confirm it stays as-is and is never expanded into an agent channel.
- Website-monitor politeness: minimum check interval / per-host rate so scheduled fetches never hammer a target site or look abusive.
- Shared multi-agent record shape: a new Task/SharedLead model vs extending Conversation/leadStatus; and whether cross-agent read/write needs per-agent permission scoping.


### 4.11  Tool & Integration Registry + Agentic Runtime loop  `TOOL`  ·  29 tasks

This module adds an in-process, typed Tool registry that mirrors the existing channel-adapter registry, a common policy-gated execution interface, five first-party tools wrapping capabilities the product already has (channel send, CRM lead write, knowledge search, web fetch, calendar booking), and a BOUNDED tool_use loop exposed as executionMode='agentic' on top of the Phase 2 model gateway. Everything is additive around the single-turn reply loop: with no tools enabled the hot path is byte-for-byte today's, {{LINK}}/[[ALERT]]/NEEDS_HUMAN stay deterministic post-processing, and no model-emitted token ever authorizes a privileged action (every tool call re-authorizes server-side). It also delivers the owner-facing UX — an outcome-named capability/tool picker in the Studio and a "what the agent did" tool-call trace in the execution viewer with approve-before-act — plus the MCP client-only adapter kept DEFERRED behind a decision record.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **TOOL-01** | Define the typed Tool contract (tools/types.ts) | BE | 6 | P1 | Phase 4 AgentSpec module, Phase 3 policy module | Create tools/types.ts with Tool<I,O> { name, description, inputSchema (zod), outputSchema (zod), sideEffect:'read'\|'write'\|'external', permission, idempotent?, timeoutMs?, execute(input, ctx) }, a ToolContext { agent, orgId, conversationId } shape, and a structured ToolResult error contract. Keep it MCP-shaped (name + JSON-Schema input + structured result) so a later MCP client adapter fits the same contract. **Done:** Types compile and export; a sample tool's zod inputSchema converts to an Anthropic tool JSON Schema via zod-to-json-schema; sideEffect/permission are enumerated and required. |
| **TOOL-02** | In-process Tool registry mirroring channels/registry.ts | BE | 6 | P1 | TOOL-01 | Create tools/registry.ts with registerTool/getTool/listTools over a Map, mirroring channels/registry.ts exactly. Tools self-register at module load; duplicate names are rejected so adding a tool is one register() call with no core change. **Done:** Registering two tools then getTool/listTools returns them; a duplicate name throws; listTools() is the single source the picker and gateway read from. |
| **TOOL-03** | Common tool execution interface (tools/execute.ts) | BE | 6 | P1 | TOOL-01, TOOL-02, Phase 3 policy/approvals module, Phase 2 model-gateway/trace module | Create tools/execute.ts: validate input against the tool's zod schema, gate by side-effect class (write/external route through policy authorize(ctx,action) + approve-before-send; read executes directly), enforce per-tool timeoutMs with abort, one retry on transient error, in-execution idempotency, output-schema validation, the error contract, and exactly one structured pino log line per execution. **Done:** A 'read' tool executes inline; a 'write'/'external' tool creates an Approval and does not act until approved; a timeout aborts and surfaces the error contract; a transient failure retries exactly once; exactly one structured log line is emitted with no secrets/PII. |
