When tools is omitted the request is byte-for-byte today's single-turn call. **Done:** callModel({...}) with no tools produces an identical request/response to today's askClaude path; with tools it surfaces tool_use blocks as structured toolCalls; the mapping is covered by a unit test. |
| **TOOL-05** | Bounded tool_use loop as executionMode='agentic' | BE | 6 | P1 | TOOL-03, TOOL-04, Phase 2 runtime-facade module | Add the agentic sibling path in engine/runtime.ts: when executionMode='agentic', run a bounded loop — call gateway with the agent's enabled tools, on tool_use dispatch to tools/execute.ts, append tool_result, re-call; terminate on end_turn, a hard step cap, or an overall timeout. Default executionMode='reply' dispatches the unchanged processInbound path (tools omitted => byte-for-byte today). **Done:** A reply-mode agent is byte-identical to today for the same input; an agentic agent completes a 2-tool scenario within the step cap; hitting the cap or timeout terminates gracefully with a safe fallback reply rather than looping. |

Enforce the non-negotiable rule that no model-emitted token authorizes a privileged action — every tool call is re-authorized server-side via the policy engine regardless of model output. **Done:** In agentic mode [[ALERT]] still dedupes+notifies the owner and {{LINK:Name}} still substitutes exact URLs; a crafted model text cannot trigger a write/external action without passing authorize(); unit test asserts re-authorization on every tool call. |

| **TOOL-24** | Tests: builtin tools (happy + guard paths) | Test | 6 | P1 | TOOL-07, TOOL-08, TOOL-09, TOOL-10, TOOL-11 | Tests that channel.send_message delegates to the right adapter; crm tools write only within orgId (cross-tenant denied); knowledge.search returns expected matches; web.fetch_page refuses private/metadata IPs; calendar.book requires approval and prevents double-booking. **Done:** Each builtin tool has a happy-path and a guard-path test, all green; the cross-tenant CRM write test returns no rows; the SSRF test rejects 169.254.169.254. |
| **TOOL-25** | Docs: 'Adding a tool' guide + reconcile architecture docs | Docs | 6 | P2 | TOOL-01, TOOL-03, TOOL-05 | Write a short guide showing that adding a tool is one tools/builtin/ module + a register() call with no core-engine change, document the non-negotiable rule (no model token authorizes a privileged action) and the executionMode='reply' default, and reconcile DATA_MODEL.md/ARCHITECTURE.md to describe the tool registry + agentic mode that now exist. **Done:** A new dev can add a read-only tool by following the guide; the docs describe the registry, the bounded loop, the side-effect/approval model, and the MCP-shaped contract as built (not aspirational). |
| **TOOL-26** | MCP client-only adapter behind the registry (DEFERRED, decision record) | Docs | 7 | P3 | TOOL-01 | Do NOT build an MCP mesh now and never expose the platform as an MCP server. Record a decision with the explicit flip trigger (a specific external/community MCP server is actually needed) and its preconditions (SSRF allowlist + encrypted secrets store + per-tenant cost caps), and verify the Tool contract stays MCP-shaped so a single McpToolAdapter can later consume external servers behind the same registry. **Done:** A written decision record names the flip trigger and preconditions; no MCP server is exposed; a check confirms the Tool contract is MCP-shaped (name + JSON-Schema input + structured result). |
| **TOOL-27** | Tool credential binding via ConnectedAccount (for calendar/auth tools) | Security | 6 | P2 | TOOL-03, TOOL-11, Phase 3 ConnectedAccount/secrets module | For tools that need auth (e.g. calendar.book), bind a credential from the Phase 3 encrypted ConnectedAccount store: a tool declares a required provider/scope and tools/execute.ts injects the decrypted credential into ToolContext only at call time, never into prompts or logs. An optional Integration row maps agent->provider credential. **Done:** calendar.book reads its credential from ConnectedAccount decrypted at execution; a missing/invalid credential yields a friendly 'connect your calendar' path instead of a crash; credentials never appear in prompts, traces, or logs. |
| **TOOL-28** | Boot wiring + feature flag for tool self-registration | Infra | 6 | P1 | TOOL-02, TOOL-07, TOOL-08, TOOL-09, TOOL-10, TOOL-11 | Import tools/builtin/* at startup (mirroring channel adapter registration) so tools self-register, behind a flag/gating that keeps the hot path unchanged when off. With no tools enabled and executionMode='reply', startup and the reply path are byte-for-byte today's. **Done:** With the flag off / no agent tools enabled, boot and the reply path are byte-for-byte today; when builtin modules are imported they appear in registry.listTools(); registration happens once at startup like channel adapters. |
| ✚ **TOOL-29** | Per-tool-call step persistence store for the agentic execution trace | DB | 6 | P2 | OBS-1, DB-3, TOOL-16 | Add the backing store that TOOL-16 writes per-tool-call step rows into, as a real Prisma migration — either a new ExecutionStep table (executionId, stepIndex, toolName, redacted inputJson, outcome, latencyMs, costMicros, approvalState, createdAt; orgId/agentId scoped) OR an additive stepsJson column on the Phase-2 execution-trace row. The Phase-2 trace (OBS-1/MGW-4 extended UsageEvent) is exactly ONE row per run with no per-step field, and Phase 6 does not formally depend on Phase 5 (so EWS-04 WorkflowStepLog cannot be assumed), leaving agentic tool-call steps with no home. Make the table-vs-JSON and reuse-WorkflowStepLog-or-not decision explicit and migrate it. (Audit 9 labeled this TOOL-30; assigned TOOL-29 as the first id past TOOL's max; its proposed TOOL-29 dry-run is merged into STU-51.) **Done:** A migration adds the per-tool-call step store via prisma migrate deploy with no data loss; TOOL-16 writes ordered steps keyed to executionId; a reply-mode run writes zero step rows; steps are orgId/agent-scoped and queryable by the execution viewer (TOOL-18); a short decision note records table-vs-JSON and why WorkflowStepLog was/was not reused given Phase 6 does not depend on Phase 5. |

**UX coverage —** Capability/Tool picker in the agent Studio/SetupGuide: outcome-named toggles ('Let it book appointments', 'Let it look up your price list'), zero-decision safe defaults (read-only on, write/external off), <=3 controls before primary, one disclosure stating defaults in words; Tool-call trace in the execution viewer: a plain-language 'what the agent did' step list per agentic reply, surfaced in ChatsPanel / an execution detail view off GET /api/analytics/executions/:id; Approve-before-act surface: pending write/external tool calls (send, calendar.book, outbound) appear in the generalized Approval inbox with a 'show what changed' summary and one-tap approve/reject; Test/Simulate console shows the tool calls an agentic agent would make before go-live (reuses TestPanel / draftFor), so owners verify by chatting not configuring; GET /api/agents/:id/tools catalog surfaced as the picker's data source (kills jargon; each tool shows friendly outcome, side-effect class, and whether it needs approval).

**Open questions —**
- calendar.book provider for v1: OAuth2 Google Calendar is explicitly deferred (Phase 7 flip trigger), so should v1 ship against a manual/internal slot store (ConnectedAccount-less) and defer the real calendar connector, or hold calendar.book until the OAuth connector lands?
- Ownership boundary: do pgvector KnowledgeChunk, ContactMemory, and the rolling-conversation-summary belong to this Tool module (because knowledge.search is the seam) or to a dedicated Memory & Knowledge module? Flagged to avoid double-ownership — this backlog scopes only the keyword/LIKE knowledge.search and its pgvector-ready signature.
- Durable idempotency for external tool sends (channel.send_message, calendar.book): reuse the Phase 5 outbound send-ledger (OutboundSendLog) idempotency key, or add a dedicated per-tool idempotency table? This module assumes in-execution idempotency + reuse of the Phase 5 ledger for cross-run dedupe.
- Minor (per locked decisions, not expanding Cloud API for agents): OTP continues to use Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender; no agent tool in this module touches Cloud API. Flagging only that calendar/web tools must not accidentally become a Cloud-API outbound path.


### 4.12  Memory & Knowledge / RAG  `MK`  ·  39 tasks

This module turns today's "stuff the whole training set into every prompt + last-8 raw messages" approach into a bounded, scoped, retrieval-on-trigger memory system that never regresses the single-turn reply loop. It defines five isolated memory scopes (conversation, agent-knowledge, per-contact, operational, shared-org) with a hard "never auto-share across agents" invariant, adds a fail-safe token-budget guard so prompt-stuffing degrades gracefully as a knowledge base grows, introduces per-contact memory (ContactMemory) and a rolling conversation summary, and layers pgvector-in-Postgres RAG (KnowledgeChunk + embeddings + re-embed pipeline + knowledge.search swap) plus file/document upload ingestion — all gated behind an explicit trigger (KB exceeds the prompt budget, or a PDF/catalog upload) and flagged off by default so small-tenant behaviour stays byte-identical. The owner-facing centerpiece is a plain-language "What your agent knows" view with per-source enable/disable + delete and a per-contact memory view, keeping the invisible-UI, zero-decision-default philosophy.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **MK-1** | Define the five memory scopes + isolation contract | Docs | 6 | P1 | — | Write the governing spec that enumerates the five memory scopes (conversation, agent-knowledge, per-contact, operational, shared-org), their store, retention/TTL, owner-visibility/deletability, and the hard invariant that memory is never auto-shared across agents (shared-org requires explicit opt-in). This contract is what every later task implements and the isolation test-suite asserts against. **Done:** A committed doc defines all five scopes with store, isolation boundary, retention/TTL, and owner controls; 'never auto-share across agents' is stated as a testable invariant referenced by MK-33. |
| **MK-2** | Prompt token/size budget estimator | BE | 6 | P1 | — | Add a pure helper that estimates the token/char size of a composed prompt (composeSystemPrompt output + conversation window) against a configurable budget, returning measured size and over/under. Char/4 estimate is acceptable v1; upgrade to exact counts from the model gateway when available. **Done:** Unit test: given known profile+sources+history, the estimator returns a stable size and the correct over/under verdict for a configured budget. |
| **MK-3** | Fail-safe budget guard in prompt composition + RAG-eligible flag | BE | 6 | P1 | MK-2, DB (Phase 1 migrations), Observability/model-gateway (Phase 2) | Wire the estimator into prompt composition so that when enabled sources exceed the budget, trimming is deterministic and priority-ordered (always keep instructions/restrictions; drop lowest-priority facts/FAQs last) and the prompt never silently overflows. Set a per-agent 'over-budget / RAG-eligible' flag and emit a structured warning so the owner can be told and the RAG trigger can fire. **Done:** With a deliberately oversized KB, the composed prompt stays at/under the cap, instructions+restrictions survive, the agent is flagged RAG-eligible, and a warning is logged; a small KB is untouched (byte-identical). |
| **MK-4** | RAG trigger decision record + detector | BE | 6 | P1 | MK-3 | Record, as a written decision, the exact conditions that flip an agent from prompt-stuffing to retrieval (training exceeds the prompt budget, or a PDF/catalog/document is uploaded) and implement the detector that sets/clears the agent's RAG mode on those triggers. Honors the 'adopt pgvector only on a recorded trigger' stance. **Done:** A decision record names both triggers; crossing the budget or uploading a document sets Agent.ragMode, and dropping back under budget (with no documents) is handled explicitly; no tenant is put in RAG mode without a trigger. |
| **MK-5** | Conversation rolling-summary columns | DB | 6 | P1 | DB (Phase 1 migrations) | Add additive columns to Conversation for a rolling summary (summary text, summaryUpdatedAt, and a lastSummarizedMessageId/summaryMsgCount watermark) via a real migration. No behaviour change until MK-6/MK-7 use them. **Done:** Migration adds nullable summary columns to Conversation and applies via `prisma migrate deploy`; existing rows and reply behaviour are unchanged. |
| **MK-6** | Rolling conversation-summary generator | BE | 6 | P1 | MK-5, Observability/model-gateway (Phase 2) | Add a generator that, when a conversation exceeds N messages, summarizes the older messages into Conversation.summary via the model gateway (askClaudeText), incrementally and idempotently, keeping the most recent K messages raw. It runs fire-and-forget and must never block or fail the reply path. **Done:** A >N-message conversation produces a summary covering messages up to the watermark; a second call only summarizes new messages; a summarization error leaves the conversation usable and logs without throwing into processInbound. |
| **MK-7** | Swap engine history from raw take:8 to summary + recent window | BE | 6 | P1 | MK-6 | Replace the raw `take:8` history assembly in processInbound and draftFor with 'rolling summary + recent-window' context: below the summary threshold the exact same recent messages are used (byte-identical), above it the summary plus the last K raw messages are used. This is the conversation-scope memory upgrade. **Done:** For a short conversation the assembled history string is identical to today's take:8 output; for a long conversation it contains the summary + last K messages and stays within the budget guard. |
| **MK-8** | Tests: conversation summary + unchanged short-chat path | Test | 6 | P1 | MK-7 | Add tests covering: short conversations reply byte-identically, long conversations include the summary, the summary regenerates incrementally, and a summary failure falls back to the raw recent window. Uses mocked model calls (per the Phase 0 askClaude mock). **Done:** Test-suite proves short-chat history is unchanged, long-chat uses the summary, incremental regeneration works, and summary failure degrades to the recent window. |
| **MK-9** | ContactMemory table (per-contact facts + rolling summary + TTL) | DB | 6 | P1 | DB (Phase 1 migrations), MK-1 | Add a ContactMemory model (orgId, agentId, conversationId/contactNumber, factsJson, summary, expiresAt/TTL, createdAt, updatedAt; unique per agent+contact) via migration. Carries orgId+agentId for direct tenant isolation and is scoped so one agent's contact memory is never visible to another. **Done:** Migration creates ContactMemory with orgId+agentId+contact unique key and expiresAt; a row is only reachable via its owning agent+org. |
| **MK-10** | Per-contact memory extraction/update pipeline | BE | 6 | P2 | MK-9, Observability/model-gateway (Phase 2) | After a conversation turn (or on a cadence), extract durable per-contact facts (name, stated preferences, prior interest) and a rolling per-contact summary into ContactMemory via the model gateway, with a TTL refresh. Fire-and-forget, opt-outable per contact, and strictly single-agent/single-contact. **Done:** A contact who states a durable fact has it captured in ContactMemory with a refreshed expiresAt; extraction never writes to another agent's/contact's row and never blocks the reply. |
| **MK-11** | Inject ContactMemory into prompt context under the budget guard | BE | 6 | P2 | MK-10, MK-3 | When present and not expired, add this agent+contact's ContactMemory (facts + summary) to the composed prompt, subject to the MK-3 budget guard, clearly separated from untrusted customer text. Isolation: only the current agent+contact's memory is ever loaded. **Done:** A returning contact's reply reflects their stored memory; the memory block respects the budget guard; a different contact on the same agent sees none of it. |
| **MK-12** | ContactMemory TTL sweeper | Infra | 6 | P2 | MK-9, Event/Workflow spine (Phase 5) | Add a periodic sweep that deletes expired ContactMemory rows, reusing the Phase 5 node-cron ticker/job spine if available, otherwise a minimal scheduled task behind a flag. Keeps per-contact memory bounded and honors the TTL promise. **Done:** Rows past expiresAt are removed on the next sweep; the sweep is idempotent and leaves non-expired rows intact. |

All go through the policy chokepoint and write an audit entry. **Done:** Owner can read and delete a contact's memory via the API; a member without permission is denied; deletion removes the row and writes an AuditLog entry; cross-tenant access 404s. |

Retrieval adds only bounded latency and leaves the common single-call path unchanged. **Done:** An under-budget agent's prompt is byte-identical to today; an over-budget/RAG agent's prompt contains retrieved chunks and stays within budget; one model call on the common path is preserved. |

The owner-facing centerpiece of the module. **Done:** Owner sees everything the agent knows grouped by type, can enable/disable or delete any source in one tap with a 'what changed' confirmation, reads a plain-language summary, and sees a clear over-budget prompt to enable retrieval. |

Removes the false 'already implemented' promises flagged in current-state.md. **Done:** DATA_MODEL.md and TRAINING.md no longer claim unimplemented pgvector/RAG/RLS as present; they describe the on-trigger plan and reference the scope contract and target-architecture doc. |

MK-4 (detector) sets Agent.ragMode and MK-34 gates behind per-agent opt-in, but neither provides the schema/migration and MK-4 does not depend on Phase-1 migrations; every other new MK column/table (MK-5/9/16/23) has a dedicated DB task and this one is missing. **Done:** Migration adds nullable ragMode + per-agent memory feature-flag columns to Agent and applies via prisma migrate deploy; existing rows default to flags-off so loadTraining/composeSystemPrompt output is byte-identical; MK-4/MK-11/MK-21/MK-29/MK-34 read these columns rather than defining them inline. |

MK-23 (the only TrainingSource DB task) is scoped strictly to the document type + byte storage and excludes these; MK-18 'tracks per-source status' and MK-30 marks a source 'org-shared' as BE tasks with no migration home. **Done:** Migration adds nullable status/statusError/retryCount and a sharedOrg boolean (default false) to TrainingSource via prisma migrate deploy; existing fact/link/faq rows default to ready+unshared with no behaviour change; MK-18/24/27/30 read/write these columns instead of defining them inline. |
| ✚ **MK-38** | Embeddings-provider + data-residency/DPDP decision record | Docs | 6 | P1 | MK-1 | Record, as a written decision (parallel to MK-4's RAG-trigger record), the concrete embeddings backend for the gateway's embed() path, since the locked decisions leave it unspecified and Anthropic has no first-party embeddings endpoint. Resolve provider choice (e.g. Voyage AI vs other/self-hosted), per-tenant cost fit against the budget gate, and the data-residency/DPDP posture for sending tier-2 Indian SMB training + customer text (and extracted ContactMemory facts) to a third-party vendor. An unowned prerequisite for MK-17 (impl-only), currently only an open question. **Done:** A committed decision record names the embeddings provider, its cost model against per-tenant budget, and the data-residency/DPDP/retention stance for content leaving the platform; it links MK-17 and target-architecture §8; MK-17 implements against the named provider with no remaining open question. |
| ✚ **MK-39** | Backfill chunk+embed of existing enabled sources on RAG-mode flip | BE | 6 | P1 | MK-4, MK-18 | Add the orchestration that, when MK-4 flips an agent into ragMode because its existing facts/FAQs/links exceed the prompt budget (no new upload), enqueues MK-18's chunk+embed pipeline for every currently-enabled TrainingSource of that agent. Without it the primary RAG trigger leaves the agent in ragMode with zero KnowledgeChunk rows, so MK-21 retrieval returns nothing and the agent silently loses its knowledge; MK-18 only embeds on new-source ingestion/edit and MK-4 only sets the flag. **Done:** Flipping an agent into ragMode via the budget trigger enqueues ingestion jobs for all enabled sources; once ready, MK-21 retrieval has chunks covering the full prior KB and no enabled source is missing; the backfill is idempotent (re-flip does not duplicate chunks) and never blocks replies. |

**UX coverage —** 'What your agent knows' per-agent view (evolves TrainingPanel.tsx's knowledge card into a dedicated screen): every source grouped by type with per-source enable/disable toggle + delete, plain-language summary, and a 'switch on smart retrieval' banner when the KB is over budget; Document/file upload flow in the knowledge view (drag-drop or pick a PDF/CSV/catalog; live per-document status Queued/Processing/Ready/Failed+Retry; enable/disable/delete each document); Per-contact memory view inside the conversation/lead detail (ChatsPanel.tsx / Leads.tsx): 'What this agent remembers about this contact' facts + rolling summary, with 'Forget this contact' delete and a visible TTL/expiry; Smart-retrieval (RAG) enablement as a zero-decision default with an owner-facing 'show what changed' confirmation when an agent flips from prompt-stuffing to retrieval; Share-knowledge-across-agents control (explicit opt-in only; a 'uses shared knowledge' indicator on agents that reference org-shared sources); Source status + trust indicators (link/document fetch status, 'trimmed because over budget' notice) consistent with the invisible-UI copy rules in UX_PRINCIPLES.md.

**Open questions —**
- Embeddings provider is unspecified by the locked decisions: Anthropic has no first-party embeddings endpoint, so the provider-abstracted embed() in the model gateway needs a concrete backend (e.g. Voyage AI, which Anthropic recommends, or another vendor/self-hosted). Which provider, and does its cost fit the per-tenant budget and data-residency expectations for tier-2 India?
- Document-byte storage location: local Railway volume vs object storage (S3-style) — affects DR, the single-container model, and whether RemoteAuth-style volumes are already assumed. Where do uploaded PDFs/catalogs live, and what are the size/count caps per plan?
- ContactMemory default + privacy: is per-contact memory ON by default or opt-in, and what is the default TTL? (DPDP / consent implications for storing durable facts about end customers who message a business.)
- Is pgvector guaranteed available on the production Railway Postgres (extension installable), and do we keep a keyword/LIKE fallback permanently for SQLite dev, or require Postgres for any RAG-enabled tenant?
- Who bears embedding/summarization token spend within plan limits — is it metered into the same per-tenant cost cap as replies, and should re-embedding on every source edit be rate-limited to avoid cost spikes?


### 4.13  UX System & Cross-cutting Experience  `UX`  ·  38 tasks

This module owns the app-wide invisible-UI design system, the information architecture, and every cross-cutting experience (first-run/empty/loading/error states, realtime status, optimistic updates, approvals inbox, notifications, accessibility, i18n, theming, PWA/Capacitor chrome) that ties the agentic platform together into a cohesive "personal agents" product. It is additive around the existing React/Vite/wouter/react-query client (client/src/) and aligns every screen to docs/UX_PRINCIPLES.md so that anyone can create and run their own agent with the fewest clicks. It deliberately does NOT re-implement the Studio creation flow or the Workflow/Observability/Policy backends; instead it provides the shell, navigation slots, state surfaces, and presentation those modules plug into, and it grows phase-by-phase as their APIs land.

**Open questions —**
- Agent-detail tab taxonomy: should 'Create/edit' be a tab inside AgentDetail, or stay a gallery-level flow owned by Studio with detail showing only Connect/Train/Test/Chats/Automations/Insights? This changes UX-09 and the nav.
- Insights placement: global /insights page, per-agent detail tab, or both? Affects UX-20 and the navigation IA (UX-08).
- Notifications source of truth: is there a backend notifications store/endpoint planned, or should the feed stay client-derived from leads + approvals + ws events? Affects UX-16's scope.
- i18n v1 scope: translate only product chrome now (agent replies already follow training), and is Telugu/Hindi copy owner-provided or to be drafted? Affects UX-31 effort.
- Per locked decisions, OTP delivery still uses Meta Cloud API at the platform level — confirmed no agent-facing UX change is needed for signup/reset; flag only if that sender is swapped (noted as a minor open question, not expanding Cloud API for agents).


### 4.14  Testing, CI/CD & Validation  `QA`  ·  59 tasks

This module builds the test harness, CI/CD guards, and NFR-validation drills that let every other module ship additively without regressing the working reply loop. Its spine is a mock-askClaude harness plus an in-memory channel adapter so the money/safety path (auto-send, NEEDS_HUMAN, [[ALERT]], {{LINK}}) finally has tests before brain.ts becomes a gateway, an isolated test DB that can never touch the live Railway Postgres that server/.env points at, and a GitHub Actions pipeline that enforces typecheck+tests, real Prisma migrations, and "no --accept-data-loss on prod". It then adds per-phase test suites (policy default-deny, approval idempotency, workflow runner restart/DLQ, outbound-drip idempotency, tool-execute gating, E2E for the five locked scenarios) and the measured NFR drills (RSS/agent ceiling, second-replica break, DR restore) the target architecture insists be proven rather than claimed.

| ID | Title | T | Ph | Pri | Depends | Detail & acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **QA-01** | Isolated test DB + hard prod-DB guard | Infra | 0 | P0 | — | Add server/vitest.config.ts and tests/setup.ts that force DATABASE_URL to an ephemeral per-run SQLite file and push the schema before tests, then teardown. Hard-fail the run if DATABASE_URL resolves to a postgres/remote host, because server/.env currently points at the live Railway prod DB. **Done:** `npm test` never opens a connection to Railway; a run started with DATABASE_URL set to any postgres:// URL aborts before the first test with a clear message; existing api.test.ts/unit.test.ts stay green on the ephemeral SQLite DB. |
| **QA-02** | mock-askClaude / model-gateway test double | Test | 0 | P0 | QA-01 | Create tests/helpers/mockClaude.ts that vi.mocks ../src/lib/brain (and later lib/model-gateway) with scripted replies: plain text, a leading [[ALERT]] line, NEEDS_HUMAN, a {{LINK:Name}} token, empty, and refusal. This is the prerequisite the gap analysis flags as 'land mock-askClaude before the gateway refactor'. **Done:** A test exercises processInbound/draftFor with no network and no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, and can deterministically drive each reply variant. |
| **QA-03** | In-memory mock channel adapter + fixture factories | Test | 0 | P0 | QA-01 | Add tests/helpers/mockAdapter.ts (a ChannelAdapter that records send() calls instead of launching Chromium) and factories.ts (makeOrg/makeAgent/makeTraining/makeConversation). De-duplicates setup across every engine, outbound and E2E suite and keeps puppeteer out of tests. **Done:** Suites register the mock adapter via channels/registry and assert captured outbound payloads; no test spawns a browser; factories produce valid rows for all required Prisma fields. |

This is the one Phase-0 live-hole fix (FND-19) with no test, though every other Phase-0 hole has one (QA-07/08/09/10/25). **Done:** A cross-origin request with a disallowed Origin receives no matching Access-Control-Allow-Origin header; an allowlisted Origin is echoed; the test fails against today's app.ts `origin: origins.length ? origins : true` fallback and passes after FND-19 lands. |
| ✚ **QA-57** | orgId-backfill data-migration correctness test (populate + no row loss, batched Message path) | Test | 1 | P0 | QA-01, QA-13, DB-7, DB-8, DB-9, DB-10 | Validate the Phase-1 orgId backfill as a DATA migration, not just a schema apply: seed pre-backfill rows on ChannelSession/TrainingProfile/TrainingSource/Message (some belonging to different orgs, reachable only transitively via agentId), run the backfill migration, then assert every row's orgId is populated correctly, no rows are dropped/duplicated, and the batched Message backfill completes over a volume that triggers batching. The roadmap Phase-1 risks warn 'the first migration can drop columns' and 'the Message backfill needs batching'; QA-13 only applies migrations to an EMPTY Postgres and QA-26/DB-13 only test isolation reads after the fact, so backfill data-correctness is unrepresented. **Done:** Seeded pre-backfill rows across all four tables end with correct orgId and identical row counts after the backfill; the batched Message path processes a multi-batch dataset without loss; a deliberately broken backfill (leaving a table un-populated or dropping rows) fails the test. Runs against the ephemeral Postgres from QA-13 so the batched SQL is exercised on the prod dialect. |
| ✚ **QA-58** | Locked-decision compliance guard test (no Cloud-API agent transport / no flight / no LinkedIn) | Security | 1 | P1 | QA-11, PXD-3, QA-34 | A CI guard test that mechanically asserts the forbidden surface from locked decisions #2 and #3 never reappears: no whatsapp-cloud agent adapter in channels/registry.listAdapters() (expect exactly whatsapp-web, website, telegram), no Meta inbound route POST /api/webhooks/whatsapp and no partner /api/v1/* in routes.ts, no Campaign/CampaignRecipient/DeliveryEvent/WebhookEndpoint/ApiKey Prisma models in schema.prisma, and no flight.* or linkedin tool/adapter module. Operationalizes §5.3 so a future contributor/AI pass cannot silently re-derive the dropped BROADCAST/partner-API or flight/LinkedIn paths that the roadmap/target-arch source text still mentions. Complements PXD-3 and QA-34. (Audit 14 labeled this QA-56; renumbered to avoid the CORS-test collision.) **Done:** vitest run passes on the compliant tree; adding a whatsapp-cloud adapter, a /api/webhooks/whatsapp or /api/v1 route, any Campaign*/DeliveryEvent/WebhookEndpoint/ApiKey model, or a flight.*/linkedin module turns the test red with a clear message; runs in CI on every PR. |
| ✚ **QA-59** | Deferred design record — Postgres advisory-lock per-conversation lock paired with the session-worker split | Infra | 7 | P3 | QA-46, QA-47, QA-55, FND-30 | target-arch §13 and gap-analysis risk #6 require moving the per-conversation lock (FND-30 — an in-memory Map, single-process-only) to Postgres advisory locks IN THE SAME CHANGE as the WhatsApp session-worker extraction, or the double-reply bug Phase 0 fixed reappears across processes/replicas. QA-46/QA-47 measure the RSS ceiling and demonstrate the 2nd-replica break, and QA-55 records deferral triggers, but no task captures this specific correctness coupling or the worker-split implementation it belongs to (there is no session-worker-extraction task anywhere in the inventory). (Audit 12 labeled this QA-56; renumbered.) **Done:** A decision record ties the session-worker extraction to a Postgres-advisory-lock replacement of the per-conversation lock as a single paired change, with an acceptance that two near-simultaneous inbound messages for one contact still yield exactly one reply across processes/replicas (the FND-30/FND-41 guarantee holds post-split); explicitly gated on QA-46's measured ceiling trigger; nothing built speculatively. |

**UX coverage —** QA-53 seeded demo/fixture workspace (demo org + WhatsApp-reply, website-widget and booking agents + sample leads/conversations + a Trackly drip) for manual UX walkthroughs and E2E fixtures; QA-51 client test tooling (none today) + smoke tests for the agent-creation centerpiece: describe-in-words -> spec review -> template picker -> test console -> go-live; QA-51 approvals inbox render/submit smoke test (the invisible-UI approve-before-send surface); QA-52 automated invisible-UI budget checks: <=3 controls before the primary action, outcome-named buttons (no bare Submit/Save), 44px tap targets, per-job click-count ceilings from UX_PRINCIPLES.md; QA-40/QA-45 E2E assertions that the Test Console ('Ask your agent anything') and website widget behave as the UX spec promises.

**Open questions —**
- server/.env DATABASE_URL points at the live Railway prod Postgres (per workspace memory). QA-01 makes tests force an ephemeral SQLite DB and hard-fail on any postgres URL - confirm no CI or local workflow is expected to run tests against prod.
- Tests run on SQLite but prod is Postgres (provider sed-swapped at image build). Do we want CI to ALSO run the suite against an ephemeral Postgres (QA-13 adds migrate-apply there) to catch dialect/SQL drift, or is SQLite-for-tests + Postgres-migrate-check sufficient?
- Coverage thresholds (QA-50): what overall baseline % do we commit to beyond the money/safety-path >=90%? Need a starting number to gate CI without blocking Phase 0.
- OTP currently uses the Meta Cloud API as a platform sender; should we add a test/ingest for OTP delivery-status webhooks, or leave Meta webhooks entirely out of the test surface (keeping Cloud API strictly as auth infra, not an agent channel)?
- Client has zero test tooling today (QA-51 adds Vitest + RTL). Confirm appetite for introducing a frontend test stack now vs deferring client tests to the Agent Studio FE module.
- NFR drills QA-46/47/48 need access to the real Railway instance and a Postgres backup to run - who provisions the shadow DB and the measurement window?


### 4.15  Platform Extensibility & Documentation (cross-cutting)  `PXD`  ·  36 tasks

This module makes the platform provably extensible and keeps the repo's documentation honest and authoritative. It (1) documents and enforces the five extension seams — channel adapter, tool, workflow handler, trigger, role template — so a new capability is "one module + one register() call, zero core-engine change"; (2) stands up a decision log that records every deferred standard (OAuth, pgvector, MCP, Redis, worker-split, RLS, OTel) with a concrete flip trigger plus the explicit SKIP list; (3) reconciles the drifted docs (README, AGENTS, DATA_MODEL, TRAINING, ARCHITECTURE, BACKLOG, DEV, BROADCAST) to the real server/+client/ architecture with docs/architecture/* as the single source of truth; and (4) ships the developer guide for adding an agent capability, the CLAUDE.md standing constraints, and a future/flagged template-marketplace + "share a template" seam.

Seed it with the two seams that exist today (channel adapter, role template) and evolve it as the others land. **Done:** EXTENDING.md exists, names the register()/registry pattern per seam against real files, states the no-core-change invariant, and every other seam task links back to it. |

**UX coverage —** Add-agent gallery is generated from the channel registry (registry.listAdapters()/GET /api/channels) so a newly registered channel appears as an outcome-named tile with zero client code change; Vertical/role template picker (one-tap apply from lib/templates.ts today; future role presets) documented as the extensibility surface for the agent-creation centerpiece; Future, feature-flagged 'Share this setup as a template' entry point in the template picker (coming-soon, no backend) honoring invisible-UI: outcome-named, zero-decision, no tours; Optional hidden developer 'what's registered' introspection view listing channels/tools/triggers/handlers/templates; The developer guide carries the invisible-UI acceptance bar (<=3 controls, zero-decision defaults, show-what-changed) into every new capability so extensions ship UX-compliant; Guardrail that the client never hardcodes a channel/template catalog, preserving the data-driven 'anyone can add a capability' promise.

**Open questions —**
- Should EXTENDING.md and the decision log live under docs/architecture/ (recommended, to keep that folder the single source of truth) or at docs/ root?
- OTP delivery today uses the Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender (signup/reset) — confirm this stays as auth infrastructure and is explicitly excluded from the 'no Cloud-API for agents' decision in the decision log (minor open question per locked decision #2).
- Do agent 'role presets' (sales/support/booking/follow-up, a second dimension over vertical templates) ship as code in lib/templates.ts now, or wait for the deferred templates-as-data Template table?
- Is the read-only registry-introspection endpoint (GET /api/_meta/registries) worth building, or should seam completeness be enforced by tests only?
- Exact task-id prefixes of the sibling modules (Channels, Tools, Workflow/Events, Agent Spec & Studio, Policy/Security, Infra/CI) so cross-module dependsOn ids can be made precise.


---

## 5. Gap-audit result — additions, ownership & sequencing

A 15-agent coverage audit cross-checked all 479 tasks against the roadmap, target-architecture and gap-analysis. It confirmed broad coverage and found **33 genuinely-missing tasks** (merged above, marked ✚) — mostly backend/API seams the UI already assumed (widget-key endpoint, Watches/Booking/Outbound CRUD, per-tool-call store, memory/RAG columns), cross-phase reconciliations (the hardened token vs /ws handshake, budget gate on the reply path, lifecycleStatus backfill, secret redaction in spec snapshots), and the north-star completers (NL non-reply specs, simulate-anything, one funnel, member access).

### 5.2 Ownership resolutions (so nothing is built twice)

- AgentSpec / versioning / lifecycle foundation: SPEC OWNS it (SPEC-1..SPEC-18, SPEC-24, SPEC-26) — lib/agentSpec.ts zod schema + validateSpec + specFromAgent + applySpec, the AgentVersion + currentVersionId + lifecycleStatus migration, publish/rollback/list-versions/spec/apply-spec/clone endpoints, and lib/lifecycle.ts. STU CONSUMES it: STU-1=SPEC-2, STU-2=SPEC-4, STU-3=SPEC-5, STU-4=SPEC-6, STU-9=SPEC-1, STU-10=SPEC-13, STU-11=SPEC-15, STU-12=SPEC-14/16, STU-13=SPEC-11/12, STU-14=SPEC-17, STU-15=SPEC-24, STU-39=SPEC-18, STU-46=SPEC-26 all become cross-references; STU keeps only the creation EXPERIENCE (NL builder, templates, pickers, wizard, gallery). The applySpec single-write-path must not be built twice.
- Agent.executionMode column: owned ONCE by MGW-4 (Phase 2). SPEC/STU/TOOL/MK consume it; STU-9 drops the executionMode add and keeps only the AgentVersion/currentVersionId/lifecycleStatus migration (itself owned by SPEC-1).
- Execution-trace surface: OBS owns it. OBS-1 is the sole owner of the additive UsageEvent trace-column migration (MGW-4 narrows to Agent.executionMode only and cross-references OBS-1 — the two land as ONE Phase-2 migration). lib/trace.ts = OBS-5, lib/log.ts pino = OBS-6, redaction = OBS-7 (MGW-6 reduces to a cross-reference). The analytics endpoints = OBS-11/12/13/14 (MGW-13 -> 'see OBS-11/12/13'); /api/health/ready = OBS-15 (MGW-14 cross-references). OBS-3 (askClaude structured result) collapses into MGW-1/MGW-3. engine.ts:168 usageEvent.create: the trace-write is OBS-8 and the gateway-call swap is MGW-9, landed in one coordinated PR.
- lib/pricing.ts: owned ONCE by MGW-5 (the cost primitive feeding budget MGW-10 and trace OBS-5); OBS-4 is a cross-reference.
- Phase-0 crypto primitives: SEC owns them. lib/crypto.ts (AES-256-GCM + keyId rotation) = SEC-1; FND-16 cross-references. Telegram botToken encrypt-in-place = SEC-2; FND-17 cross-references. Bearer-token hash-at-rest = SEC-3; FND-18 cross-references. One crypto module, one bearer-hash stopgap — scheduled in the FND Phase-0 window.
- authorize() + permission matrix + middleware/authz.ts: POL owns it (POL-01 matrix/default-deny, POL-02 authorize(), POL-03 requireRole/requirePermission). SEC-10 CONSUMES POL-03 and owns only the member-vs-owner policy-table entries; SEC-11 cross-references POL-04 instead of re-gating. One middleware/authz.ts. The 'every mutating route is authz-gated' CI meta-test is owned once (consolidate POL-18 and SEC-12).
- Channel-aware prompt (engine.buildPrompt + training.composeSystemPrompt): owned ONCE by SCN-01 (Phase 0); MGW-12 absorbs/no-ops.
- Razorpay webhook: EWS-16/EWS-17 is the permanent Razorpay-ONLY raw-body webhooks router and ABSORBS/RELOCATES the FND-20 Phase-0 route (never double-mounted). FND-20 is the interim stopgap only. The router stays Razorpay-only — no Meta inbound (hub.verify_token) handler, ever.
- Approval queue: POL owns it (POL-06 table, POL-07 service, POL-08 API, POL-10 executors). All consumers register executors / park against POL rows and never create a second approval table: EWS-19/EWS-23, SCN-11, SCN-22, TOOL-11/TOOL-19, SPEC-19 (interim owner-check until POL lands), new SCN-40, SCN-41 and STU-53.
- Document upload endpoint + ingestion pipeline: owned ONCE by MK-24/MK-25/MK-26 (validate type/size -> extract -> store source -> enqueue chunk+embed). STU-43/44/45 cross-reference it — no second endpoint or pipeline.
- knowledge.search: TOOL-09 owns the tool contract + keyword/LIKE impl + the stable signature; MK-20 owns the pgvector backing behind that unchanged signature; MK-29 owns the single enable/disable (disabled-chunk exclusion) filter enforced identically in BOTH paths. MK owns the pgvector/embeddings/KnowledgeChunk/ContactMemory tables (MK-16/MK-9); TOOL-12 cross-references MK-16.
- calendar.book builtin tool: owned ONCE by SCN (SCN-21, which owns the Booking table the tool writes); TOOL-11 consumes/cross-references it. Dedupe the calendar.book test (SCN-25 is the owner; drop TOOL-24's duplicate calendar.book case or make it reference SCN-25).
- Tool-selection -> spec/executionMode wiring: TOOL-13 owns spec.tools[]/capabilities[] population, TOOL-14 owns executionMode gating + per-agent allowlist; STU-30 consumes them. GET /api/agents/:id/tools is owned once by TOOL-15 (STU-30 cross-references). The capability/tool PICKER is one component evolved STU-29(Ph4 placeholder) -> TOOL-17(Ph6 wired), not two.
- Retention/TTL sweeps share ONE always-on ticker = new EWS-34 (ungated by WORKFLOW_ENABLED, lands Phase 2). OBS-18 (traces/Message), POL-17 (approvals/audit), MK-12 (ContactMemory) and new EWS-33 (spine tables) each register their sweep into EWS-34 — NOT EWS-13 (the Phase-5, WORKFLOW_ENABLED-gated trigger ticker). This supersedes the backlog §5.4 'share the Phase-5 ticker' note, which is unworkable (EWS-13 is both later than and feature-gated away from its consumers).
- Shared client surfaces, one owner each: Approvals inbox = UX-14/UX-15 own the route + ApprovalCard (POL-11 re-scoped to policy/approvals backend wiring; TOOL-19 contributes the tool-action preview variant INTO UX-15; new UX-39 edit-before-send is a variant of UX-15, not a new surface). Automations/Runs = EWS-24/EWS-25 own run-detail/controls data, UX-22 owns the shell/tab. Monitors/Outbound consoles = SCN owns consoles/wizards (SCN-30, SCN-16/17), UX-23/UX-24 own dashboard shells. Insights = OBS owns the execution-viewer data components (OBS-19/20/21/22), UX-20 owns the Insights page IA + charts. Connected-accounts/Security screens = UX-26/UX-27 own the UI, SEC-14 re-scoped to the backend contract (pairs SEC-15). Agents home = UX-10 owns presentation/live-status, STU-42 limited to create/edit entry points. Navigation = UX-08's single data-driven config owns it; POL-20 and SCN-38 register entries, never a second NAV array. Realtime socket (ws.ts) = UX-17 owns the one authenticated multiplexed socket; new UX-40 'replying' emit rides it. Client session-expiry/logout = UX-04 (Phase 0) owns the flow; SEC-16 extends it (sign-out-everywhere), does not re-implement.
- DR restore drill: QA-48 owns the EXECUTED NFR drill + dated proof; DB-17 owns only the written runbook and cross-references QA-48. Restored-clone/shadow provisioning is stood up once by DB-2 and reused by DB-3/DB-17/new DB-24/QA-48.
- CI workflow file .github/workflows/ci.yml: owned by QA-11. DB-6, QA-12/13/14/15/50, FND-47, SEC-19(scan), PXD-28 and new QA-58/PXD-37 ADD jobs to the single file (each depends on QA-11), not parallel workflows.
- Money/safety mock-askClaude harness + suite: QA owns it (QA-02 harness, QA-04/QA-05 suite). FND-40 is the same Phase-0 deliverable cross-referencing QA; FND-41..46, SCN-02/03/04 are cross-references, not re-implementations. Committed-secret scanning = QA-14; SEC-19 owns log redaction only. The one redaction util = OBS-7, consumed by POL-13 (audit) and SEC-19 (logs).
- Seed/fixture script: owned once by QA-53 (test/E2E fixture workspace); DB-15 consumes it.
- Decision log: PXD-10 is the single home. EWS-31 (pg-boss), TOOL-26 (MCP client-only), SCN-26 (calendar OAuth), DB-19 (RLS), SEC-20 (OAuth2), QA-55 + new QA-59 (deferred NFR/advisory-lock), new MK-38 (embeddings/DPDP) and the new EWS-32 keep-inline ADR each contribute an entry, not a parallel log.
- DATA_MODEL.md: PXD-16 owns the baseline + phase-tagged 'planned tables' structure; each DB-adding task carries a 'document THIS phase's new tables' acceptance (mechanically enforced by new PXD-37). This supersedes the nine competing full-rewrite editors (FND-02, DB-22, SPEC-26, POL-19, MGW-20, OBS-29, SEC-18, MK-35). Phase-0 README/ARCHITECTURE/BACKLOG reconciliations are owned by PXD (PXD-14/15/18); FND-03/04/05 are the Phase-0 scheduling of those PXD tasks, not independent rewrites.
- Widget signed key: FND-12 MINTS the stateless HMAC key, FND-14 puts it in the embed snippet + widget.js, new FND-54 EXPOSES it to the authenticated owner. No key-minting table or separate key endpoint beyond those; the rotatable per-agent widgetKey column referenced as the phantom 'DB-23' stays a deferred rotation refinement, not a materialized task.
- Watches CRUD endpoint (POST/GET/DELETE /api/agents/:id/watches): owned by new SCN-42, which materializes the phantom 'SCN-27b' cited-as-covered but absent from the inventory; SCN-27 stays the Watch/Schedule data model only. If 'SCN-27b' already exists in a backlog addendum, reconcile SCN-42 to it rather than duplicate.

### 5.3 Sequencing — the must-be-first ordering

- QA-01 FIRST, before any other test task in any module: it forces an ephemeral test DB and hard-fails on any postgres:// / live-prod DATABASE_URL (server/.env points at live Railway prod). Every QA-02..QA-59, DB-13, SPEC-6/10/24 and every DB-touching test transitively depends on QA-01.
- DB migration baseline before every new table/column: DB-1 -> DB-2 (shadow) -> DB-3 (0_init baseline + 'migrate resolve --applied 0_init') -> DB-4 (entrypoint switch to migrate deploy, remove db push --accept-data-loss/--force-reset). DB-3 MUST precede DB-4 (else the first migrate deploy tries to CREATE existing prod tables). This gates ALL Phase 2-6 additions: MGW-4, OBS-1, POL-06, POL-12, SEC-4, SEC-7, SPEC-1 (STU-9), EWS-01..04, SCN-05/20/27/32, MK-9/16/23 and new MK-36/MK-37, new TOOL-29 step store, new SCN-40/41/42, new SPEC-28 backfill.
- Money/safety-path tests before the gateway refactor: FND-40/QA-02/QA-04/QA-05 land before MGW-1/MGW-3/MGW-9 and OBS-8 touch brain.ts/engine.ts; SPEC-8 (loadTraining edit, on the money path) also lands after these with the SPEC-10 regression.
- SSRF allowlist (FND-15) before any URL-using tool/feature: TOOL-10 (web.fetch_page), SCN-28 (scheduled-monitor fetch), new SCN-42 (watch create), MK-24/MK-25 (document/remote-media fetch), STU-7 (build-from-URL).
- Budget gate (MGW-10/MGW-11, Phase 2) before every spend feature: new MGW-21 (processInbound enforcement — the all-channels gap), SCN-12 (outbound spend cap), TOOL-21 (tool-loop step/cost cap), MK-32 (memory/RAG spend), STU-7 (NL build spend).
- Approval queue (POL-07/POL-08) before every approval consumer: EWS-19/EWS-23, SCN-11, SCN-22, TOOL-11/TOOL-19, new SCN-40, new SCN-41, new STU-53; SPEC-19 applies its interim owner-role check until POL lands.
- SPEC AgentSpec foundation (SPEC-1..5) before Studio pickers/tools consume it: STU-20..34 pickers, new STU-50/STU-52; TOOL-13 additionally requires the tools[]/capabilities[] element schema to be defined in lib/agentSpec.ts in Phase 6 (it is only forward-declared as [] in the Phase-4 spec).
- Phase-0 crypto (SEC-1 lib/crypto.ts + SEC-17 fail-fast key mgmt) before SEC-7/SEC-8 (ConnectedAccount + secrets facade) and SEC-2/SEC-9 (botToken encrypt/migrate). SEC-17 should fail-fast on ALL required Phase-0 secrets in prod (ENCRYPTION_KEY, widget-signing secret, /ws token secret, CORS_ORIGIN). New SPEC-27 secret-redaction rides the spec work but is fully correct only once channel secrets live outside channelConfig (SEC-9).
- Client-breaking Phase-0 coupling, ship together in one release with a grace window: FND-10/FND-11 (/ws token) + FND-50/UX-04 (graceful auth-failure); SEC-3/FND-18 (bearer-hash, one-time mass-logout) + SEC-16/UX-04 (client grace-window) + new SEC-21 (WS handshake hashed-token verify + live-socket teardown); FND-19 (non-reflecting CORS) + FND-52 setting CORS_ORIGIN + the client, guarded by new QA-56.
- New EWS-34 (shared always-on maintenance ticker) MUST land by Phase 2 and be UNGATED by WORKFLOW_ENABLED — its earliest consumer OBS-18 is Phase 2 (POL-17 Phase 3), both before the Phase-5 spine. It precedes OBS-18/POL-17/MK-12/new EWS-33, which register into it; it is NOT EWS-13 (the Phase-5 flag-gated trigger ticker).
- New EWS-32 (inbound durability) sequences in Phase 5 after EWS-03/EWS-10 + FND-30/FND-31; re-point QA-49 (NFR backpressure/durability) to depend on EWS-32 instead of QA-30, so the test follows its implementation rather than being orphaned.
- New DB-24 (prod-scale backfill rehearsal + lock measurement) gates the live-prod cutover of DB-10 (batched Message backfill) + DB-11 (NOT NULL enforce + CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY); new DB-25 (failed/partial-migration recovery runbook) sequences alongside DB-11/DB-4 (the INVALID-CONCURRENT-index-blocks-all-deploys hazard they introduce); new QA-57 (backfill correctness test) runs after DB-7..10 on QA-13's ephemeral Postgres.
- Priority inversion to fix: DB-13 (P0 tenant-isolation by-id tests) cannot pass until DB-14 (P1 scope.ts orgId filter) ships — raise DB-14 to P0, or re-spec DB-13 as an expected-fail guard that flips green when DB-14 lands.
- SEC-7 (ConnectedAccount + AES-GCM) before SCN-05/SCN-08 (OutboundConnection token store/decrypt, new SCN-40) and SEC-9 (botToken migration).
- EWS workflow spine (EWS-05..EWS-14) before SCN-09 (drip handler) and SCN-28 (monitor handler), which register ONTO the spine; SCN-07 (per-agent send mutex) before SCN-09, and the EWS runner acquires that mutex before any outbound-drip send so drips never collide with live replies. EWS-14 must also start the EWS-09 outbox-drain loop (otherwise emitted DomainEvents are never matched to Triggers).
- Memory ordering: new MK-38 (embeddings-provider + DPDP decision) before MK-17 (impl), which precedes MK-16/MK-18/MK-20; new MK-36 (Agent ragMode/flags columns) before MK-4/MK-11/MK-21/MK-29/MK-34; new MK-37 status column before MK-18/24/27 and its sharedOrg before MK-30; new MK-39 (backfill chunk+embed on RAG-mode flip) sits between MK-4 and MK-21.
- Phase-4 Studio pickers that forward-declare Phase-5/6 behavior must flip from 'coming soon' to ACTIVE when their modules land: STU-30 (Ph6 capability->tool wiring) and STU-32 (Ph5 trigger wiring) must each include the Studio UI flip, or the in-scope scenarios (website-monitor, booking, outbound) stay permanent dead-ends. New STU-50 emits the non-reply spec SHAPE in Phase 4 even though execution is gated to Phase 5/6; new STU-51 (simulate ANY agent) depends on TOOL-05/TOOL-03 (Ph6) + EWS-12 (Ph5) and go-live copy must not promise a simulation it cannot yet run for non-reply agents.
- PXD-10 (decision log) + PXD-27 (CLAUDE.md standing constraints) land first so every module honors the locked decisions; PXD-1 (EXTENDING.md) lands in Phase 0 before the later seam docs (PXD-6/7/8/26) that depend on it; new PXD-35 (TRACKLY reconcile) attaches to PXD-10/PXD-23; new PXD-34 (Idempotency-Key convention) lands with/just before POST /api/agents/build (STU-7, Phase 4) and is then adopted incrementally on publish/clone/apply-spec/campaign-start.
- Phase-7 advisory-lock coupling (new QA-59): the Postgres advisory-lock replacement of the per-conversation lock (FND-30) must land in the SAME change as the session-worker extraction, gated on QA-46's measured ceiling trigger (after QA-46/QA-47) — never built speculatively.
- Cross-phase approximations to label (not blockers): the Phase-2 intervention-rate metric (OBS-13) ships as a draft-vs-auto approximation until OBS-16 populates approvalState from the Phase-3 Approval queue; the new 'budget_exceeded' outcome (MGW-21) adds a 9th value to ProcessResult.status — update the outcome enum and the OBS-8/OBS-24 one-row-per-run test in lockstep.

### 5.4 Residual notes & open decisions

- ID-collision resolutions applied (auditors numbered independently): three auditors each proposed 'QA-56' for different tasks -> split to QA-56 (CORS test, Phase-0 audit), QA-58 (locked-decision compliance guard, QA+PXD audit), QA-59 (advisory-lock design record, §11/§13 audit); QA-57 (backfill-correctness) kept. Two auditors each proposed 'PXD-34' -> PXD-34 (Idempotency-Key, §9/§10 audit) kept; TRACKLY reconcile -> PXD-35, DEPLOY reconcile -> PXD-36, schema<->DATA_MODEL parity -> PXD-37. 'EWS-32' proposed identically by two auditors -> merged. 'Simulate ANY agent' proposed as both STU-51 and the TOOL audit's dry-run -> merged into STU-51; the TOOL audit's other task (per-tool-call step store, which it labeled TOOL-30) is assigned TOOL-29 (first id past TOOL's max of 28).
- Phantom 'SCN-27b' (Watches CRUD, POST/GET/DELETE /api/agents/:id/watches) is cited as 'already captured' by the SCN and §9/§10 audits but is ABSENT from the 479-task inventory; target-architecture §10 and roadmap Phase-5 both require it and SCN-27 is the data model only -> materialized as new SCN-42. If SCN-27b already exists in a backlog addendum not reflected in the inventory snapshot, reconcile SCN-42 to it rather than create a duplicate.
- Phantom 'DB-23' (rotatable per-agent widgetKey column) is referenced by the §9/§10 audit as an addendum follow-up but is not in the inventory; the chosen design is a STATELESS HMAC widget key (FND-12/FND-14 mint, new FND-54 exposes to owner), so a per-agent widgetKey column is only an optional rotation refinement — left as a deferred note, not materialized.
- Documentation drift, not missing tasks: target-architecture §10 and §12 still list the dropped Cloud-API surface (POST /api/webhooks/whatsapp Meta inbound, /api/v1 partner API, Campaign*/DeliveryEvent/WebhookEndpoint/ApiKey tables, BROADCAST-as-default framing) because the doc text predates the 2026-11-14 locked decisions. No new task may implement them; new QA-58 is the permanent mechanical guard and the doc-reconciliation tasks (PXD-15/PXD-23 + new PXD-35/PXD-36) scrub the stale framing. OTP keeps Meta Cloud API purely as a platform auth sender (carve-out) — left as-is unless the owner wants it off (a small added task if so).
- Owner-input decisions that ship with safe defaults (do not block execution): per-plan daily message/cost caps (MGW-10 via MGW-19 defaults); whether truly-agentless outcomes (engine.ts ACK 'ignored'/'no_agent', which return before orgId resolves and OBS-1 keeps UsageEvent.orgId required) write a trace row or are excluded from the one-row rule (OBS-8/OBS-24 — needs a nullable orgId or a sentinel, decide before OBS-24 asserts the outcome set); SQLite-dev vs Postgres-prod percentile computation for latency p50/p95 (OBS-13/MGW-13 via the DB-18 dialect-parity decision — Postgres has percentile_cont, SQLite does not).
- Acceptance details to fold into existing inventory tasks (no new task): SPEC-1 must specify the AgentVersion<->Agent cyclic-FK onDelete semantics (AgentVersion.agentId Cascade, Agent.currentVersionId SetNull) with SQLite-dev/Postgres-prod parity, or agent deletion FK-violates/orphans versions. DB-8 must add the 4th TrainingProfile write site (POST /training/apply-template at agents/router.ts:127). DB-10 must correct its Message create-site enumeration to the six engine.ts sites + the manual human-takeover reply at router.ts:212 (not the no-op test console at router.ts:170). FND-30 must install the per-conversation lock at the ENGINE level so it wraps all three inbound call sites (whatsapp.ts awaited, telegram.ts fire-and-forget, public/router.ts website), and FND-41/QA-06 must exercise the Telegram and widget paths, not only WhatsApp.
- New DB-24 (operational prod-scale rehearsal/measurement) and new QA-57 (automated backfill-correctness CI test) are complementary, not duplicates — keep both; DB-24 measures lock duration on a restored clone to answer the maintenance-window question, QA-57 is the repeatable correctness assertion on ephemeral Postgres.
- Net result of this reconciliation pass: 33 deduped new tasks (after merging EWS-32 x2, STU-51+dry-run, and splitting the QA-56 x3 / PXD-34 x2 collisions), a single-owner map covering all 24 known duplication clusters, and the full must-be-first ordering. The two highest-leverage execution gates remain QA-01 (prod-DB guard) and the DB-1->DB-4 migration baseline: per workspace memory server/.env points at live Railway prod Postgres, so nothing new should touch the DB or run a test until both land. Everything proposed honors the five locked decisions (whatsapp-web-only transport; no Cloud-API/BROADCAST/partner-API/Campaign tables; standard scenarios only; describe->spec->simulate->publish north star; modular-monolith-first with executionMode='reply' byte-for-byte default).

The module-breakdown workflow's own critic. Its "missing tasks" (§6.1) are now folded in as ✚ tasks in §4/§5; the duplication (§6.4) and sequencing (§6.5) notes are superseded by the authoritative §5.2/§5.3 but kept for traceability.

### 6.1 Missing tasks it flagged (now folded in)

| Suggested | Module | Why | Folded into |
|---|---|---|---|
| Watches CRUD API — POST/GET/DELETE /api/agents/:id/watches | SCN | Roadmap Phase 5 APIs and target-arch §10 explicitly list POST/GET/DELETE /api/agents/:id/watches. SCN has the Watch model (SCN-27), the scheduled-monitor handler (SCN-28) and the Monitors console UX (SCN-30), but NO backend CRUD endpoint to create/list/pause/delete a Watch. SCN-30 (FE) has nothing to call. Gap. | ✚ §5.1 |
| Inbound reply-path durability / backpressure (or a recorded decision to keep it inline) | EWS | target-arch §13 states 'the Postgres inbound-dispatch queue (concurrency cap + retry/backoff + dead-letter) replaces today's "a failed Claude call drops the message."' No module makes inbound processing durable — FND-30 adds only an in-memory lock and MGW-2 adds in-request retry, so a process crash mid-Claude-call still loses the inbound message. Either an optional inbound outbox/retry task or an explicit decision-record (PXD-10) that inbound stays synchronous is required; right now the NFR is neither met nor consciously deferred. | ✚ §5.1 |
| DomainEvent + WorkflowStepLog retention/prune sweep | EWS | EWS's own open question flags that DomainEvent/WorkflowStepLog growth is unbounded and needs a TTL/prune. OBS-18 prunes traces/Message, POL-17 prunes approvals/audit, MK-12 prunes ContactMemory — but no task prunes the spine's outbox/step-log tables, the highest-volume additions in Phase 5. | ✚ §5.1 |
| NL builder emits non-reply scenario specs (schedule/outbound/booking triggers + tools) — or an explicit 'NL build is reply-only' decision | STU | Locked north-star #4 = 'ANYONE can create their OWN personal agent for ANYTHING' via describe-in-words. STU-5 generateSpecFromNL only emits persona/knowledge/channel/behavior (reply agents); AgentSpec.tools/triggers/capabilities stay empty. So a monitor/outbound/booking agent can ONLY be made via the separate SCN-37 scenario wizards, not from a description. The 'describe → ANYTHING' promise is unmet for non-reply scenarios, and no task closes it. | ✚ §5.1 |
| Rotatable per-agent widget key column (Phase 1 follow-up to FND-12) | DB | FND-12 ships a stateless HMAC widget key and FND's open question defers a stored, individually-rotatable widgetKey column to Phase 1 — but no DB task schedules it. A leaked widget key currently cannot be rotated per-agent without rotating the global secret for everyone. | ✚ §5.1 |
| Capability-flag-driven UI adaptation from the adapter registry | STU | AGENTS.md §2: 'Capability flags let the UI adapt (e.g. show a location field only for channels that support it).' STU-16 adds capabilities[] to adapters and STU-18 drives the channel picker from them, but no task consumes capability flags to adapt training/compose fields per channel. Minor but named in the agent doctrine. | ✚ §5.1 |

### 6.2 UX gaps it flagged

- Agentic (tool-using) agents cannot be SIMULATED before go-live. STU-35 'Simulate/Test-before-go-live' reuses engine.draftFor, which is reply-mode only. For a booking or other agentic agent the owner cannot preview the tool actions it would take (e.g. 'it would book you for Tue 3pm' / 'it would send this drip') before publishing — violating the locked north-star's 'test/simulate before go-live' for exactly the scenarios where a dry-run matters most.
- The 'describe → ANYTHING' funnel is reply-only. Creating a monitor, outbound-drip, or booking agent requires the separate SCN-37 scenario picker + its bespoke wizards; the describe-in-words entry (STU-20) always yields a reply/persona agent. There is no single funnel that reads a free-text intent and routes to schedule/outbound/booking setup, so 'easiest possible, for anything' is only partially delivered.
- Multiple, unreconciled creation entry points: the existing channel-first Agents.tsx picker, STU-20 'describe your agent', STU-26 template gallery, and SCN-37 'what should this agent do?' scenario picker. No task designates one obvious 'start here' funnel, risking a confusing first moment that undercuts the invisible-UI/fewest-clicks promise (the STU open question flags 'lead with describe vs keep channel-first' but leaves it unresolved).
- Capability/trigger pickers ship as disabled 'coming soon' controls in Phase 4 (STU-29 capabilities, STU-31 triggers) because their real wiring is Phase 5/6. An owner picking 'book appointments' or 'watch a page for changes' during that window hits a forward-declared dead-end — tension with UX_PRINCIPLES 'no empty/dead-end states' and the create-anything promise, and no task bridges the staged gap (e.g. a waitlist/notify-me affordance).
- Edit-a-draft-before-approve may regress in the generalized Approvals inbox. Today ChatsPanel supports approve/skip AND reply-edit; POL-11/POL-15/UX-15 describe only approve/reject (+ 'show what changed'). No task preserves inline edit-then-send of a draft reply inside the new /approvals surface.
- 'Live, visible work' micro-feedback is thin. UX_PRINCIPLES §2 asks to 'show the agent acting: typing indicators on drafts, a running count of replies, a feed of what the agent did.' The owner-only execution feed (OBS-19/MGW-16/TOOL-18) covers the retrospective feed, but no task delivers the real-time 'agent is replying now' affordance (typing indicator / live reply counter) on the agent card or chat.
- Member-role day-to-day experience is under-specified. Nearly every new surface (Insights, Audit, Executions, Usage meter, metric tiles) is owner-only. A member who runs approvals and the inbox gets a markedly thinner app; no task verifies the member journey is intentionally complete rather than accidentally hollowed out by the owner-gating added across OBS/MGW/POL/SEC.

### 6.3 Decision-compliance checks

- NO module task outright violates the locked decisions — but the SOURCE roadmap/target-arch still contain forbidden items that synthesis must NOT pull back in. Roadmap Phase 5 ('the whatsapp-cloud adapter', 'POST /api/webhooks/whatsapp' Meta inbound, 'partner /api/v1/*' BROADCAST API) and target-arch §9/§10 (Campaign, CampaignRecipient, DeliveryEvent, WebhookEndpoint, ApiKey tables) describe the Cloud-API outbound/BROADCAST path. These violate locked decision #2 (BROADCAST OUT; whatsapp-web QR is the ONLY agent transport). The modules correctly dropped them (SCN-15 asserts the exclusion); keep them dropped in synthesis.
- Roadmap/target-arch §12 scenario table still includes Flight monitoring (#4) and LinkedIn marketing (#3). These violate locked decision #3 ('SKIP flight monitoring and LinkedIn DM automation entirely'). The modules correctly exclude them (SCN-39 records the exclusion). Synthesis must not re-derive flight.* or LinkedIn tasks from the plan text.
- Drift risk in EWS webhooks router: the roadmap describes it as 'raw body, hub.verify_token + HMAC + dedupe' — that signature is Meta-webhook-shaped. EWS-16/EWS-17/EWS-30 correctly scope it to Razorpay-only and explicitly forbid a Meta inbound route, but the webhooks-router task must stay Razorpay-only; adding a hub.verify_token Meta handler would create a Cloud-API agent inbound path and violate decision #2.
- Latent, not a violation: OTP keeps using the Meta Cloud API as a platform-level sender (otp.ts, confirmed the only whatsappCloud reference). This is permitted as auth infrastructure under decision #2's carve-out. Ensure no later task (e.g. any webhooks/outbound work) expands whatsappCloud into an agent channel.
- Compliant-by-construction (flagged for the synthesis to preserve): versioning stays non-mandatory (SPEC-8/STU-13 keep currentVersionId=null = live-edit default), and no task introduces Temporal/Kafka/Google-A2A/LangGraph/external-IdP (EWS-31, TOOL-26, SCN-34, SEC-10 keep them deferred/in-repo). No violation — do not let a 'publish required' or 'adopt pg-boss/Redis now' step creep in.

### 6.4 Duplications & conflicts (→ resolved authoritatively in §5.2)

- MAJOR: the entire Agent Spec + versioning foundation is duplicated between SPEC and STU. Both create lib/agentSpec.ts, the zod AgentSpec, validateSpec/applySpec/specFromAgent, the AgentVersion table + Agent.currentVersionId/lifecycleStatus migration, and the publish/rollback/list-versions/spec/apply-spec/clone endpoints and round-trip tests (SPEC-1..18,24 ≈ STU-1..15,39). Assign the foundation to ONE module (SPEC) and have STU consume it, or they will build two of everything.
- MAJOR: the Phase-2 trace/pricing/analytics layer is duplicated between MGW and OBS: extend UsageEvent (MGW-4 ≈ OBS-1), lib/trace.ts (MGW-6 ≈ OBS-5/6), lib/pricing.ts (MGW-5 ≈ OBS-4), /api/analytics/executions\|metrics (MGW-13 ≈ OBS-11/12/13), /api/health/ready (MGW-14 ≈ OBS-15), and brain.ts usage capture (MGW-1 ≈ OBS-3, also FND-31). Merge ownership of the execution-trace surface.
- Agent.executionMode column is added twice — MGW-4 (Phase 2) and STU-9 (Phase 4). Add it once in Phase 2 (MGW-4); SPEC/STU/TOOL consume it.
- lib/crypto.ts is created twice (FND-16 and SEC-1, both Phase 0); Telegram botToken encrypt is FND-17 ≈ SEC-2; bearer-token hashing is FND-18 ≈ SEC-3. One owner for the Phase-0 crypto/secret-at-rest primitives.
- middleware/authz.ts and the ungated-route gating are duplicated: POL-03/POL-04 and SEC-10/SEC-11 both create the authz middleware and both gate delete-agent/edit-training/org-rename/lead-export. POL owns authorize()+matrix; SEC-10 should consume it, not re-create it.
- Channel-aware prompt is duplicated AND phase-conflicted: SCN-01 (Phase 0) and MGW-12 (Phase 2) both rewrite engine.buildPrompt + training.composeSystemPrompt to drop the hardcoded 'WhatsApp'.
- Razorpay webhook wired twice: FND-20 (Phase 0 raw-body route) and EWS-16/17 (Phase 5 webhooks router). Reconcile so Phase 5 adopts/relocates the Phase-0 route rather than double-mounting.
- Document/file upload ingestion is two different endpoints for one feature: STU-43 (POST /api/agents/:id/training/upload) vs MK-24 (POST /api/agents/:id/training/documents). Pick one.
- GET /api/agents/:id/tools is defined by both STU-30 and TOOL-15.
- knowledge.search builtin is co-owned: TOOL-09 (keyword impl) and MK-20 (pgvector backing) both edit tools/builtin/knowledge.ts; ContactMemory + rolling-summary ownership is ambiguous between the TOOL module summary and the MK tasks (MK owns; TOOL should reference).
- Client surface duplications: Approvals.tsx (POL-11 vs UX-14/15, + TOOL-19), Automations.tsx (EWS-24/25 vs UX-22, + SCN-16/24/30), owner analytics/insights UI (OBS-19/21 vs MGW-15/16 vs UX-20/21), Connected-accounts/Security UI (SEC-14/16 vs UX-26/27 vs UX-04 session handling).
- Infra/test duplications: seed script server/prisma/seed.ts (DB-15 vs QA-53); mock-askClaude money/safety tests (FND-40 vs QA-02/04/05); CI .github/workflows/ci.yml created/edited by FND-47, DB-6, QA-11, SEC-19, PXD-28.
- Shared-file fan-out needing a single owner + per-phase edits: DATA_MODEL.md (FND-02, DB-22, SPEC-26, POL-19, MGW-20, OBS-29, SEC-18, MK-35, PXD-16), README (FND-04 vs PXD-14), ARCHITECTURE.md (FND-03/PXD-15/DB-22/FND-52), config/navigation.ts + AppShell (UX-08, POL-20, EWS-24, SCN-38, OBS-22), client/src/lib/ws.ts (FND-11/50, UX-04/17, EWS-26, POL-20, OBS-22).
- Three separate retention sweeps (OBS-18 traces/Message, POL-17 approvals/audit, MK-12 ContactMemory) should share the one Phase-5 node-cron ticker (EWS-13) rather than each standing up its own scheduler.

### 6.5 Cross-module sequencing notes (→ see §5.3)

- QA-01 (force an ephemeral test DB + hard-fail if DATABASE_URL is a postgres/prod URL) must land FIRST in Phase 0 — per workspace memory server/.env points at the live Railway prod Postgres, so every test task in every module is unsafe until QA-01 exists.
- DB Phase-1 migration baseline (DB-1/2/3/4, and removing the db-push --accept-data-loss + DB_FORCE_RESET --force-reset escape hatch in docker-entrypoint.sh) is a HARD prerequisite for every new table in Phases 2-6: MGW-4, POL-06/12, SEC-4/7, SPEC-1, EWS-01-04, SCN-05/20/27/32, MK-9/16/23, TOOL-12. Sequence DB-3 before all of them; nothing additive is safe against prod first.
- FND-40 (mock-askClaude money/safety tests) must precede FND-31 (brain usage capture) and the MGW/OBS gateway refactor (MGW-1, OBS-3) — the roadmap's non-negotiable 'land the money-path tests before any runtime refactor.'
- FND-15 (SSRF allowlist in learn.ts) is a hard prerequisite for STU-7 (build-from-URL), TOOL-10 (web.fetch_page), MK-24/25 (doc upload fetch), and SCN-28 (scheduled-monitor fetch) — each re-uses the learn fetch primitive.
- MGW-10 (per-tenant budget gate) must precede SCN-12 (outbound spend cap), TOOL-21 (agentic-loop budget), STU-7 (NL build spend), and MK-32 (memory/embedding spend).
- POL-07/08 (Approval queue) must precede EWS-19/23 (workflow approve/awaitApproval), SCN-11 (approval-gated campaign start), SCN-22 + TOOL-11 (approve-before-book), and TOOL-19 (approve-before-act surface) — all reuse the one queue.
- EWS workflow spine (Phase 5: runner, scheduler, registries) must land before SCN-09 (drip handler), SCN-28 (monitor handler), and the retention sweeps that want the ticker; the three handlers register onto it, they don't own it.
- SPEC AgentSpec foundation (Phase 4) must precede TOOL-13 (populate AgentSpec.tools[]/capabilities[]), the STU pickers, and SCN scenario specs — the spec is the shared definition everything references.
- Decide the channel-aware-prompt phase once: if SCN-01 does it in Phase 0, MGW-12 (Phase 2) must be absorbed/no-op, not a second rewrite of the same two functions.
- Doc reconciliation of DATA_MODEL.md / ARCHITECTURE.md should be a per-phase acceptance ('this phase's new tables are documented') attached to each DB-adding task, NOT nine competing full-rewrites — otherwise later-phase tables will re-introduce drift the Phase-0 pass can't yet describe.
- Resolve the creation-funnel ordering early: STU-20 (describe-in-words), STU-26 (templates), SCN-37 (scenario picker) and the existing channel-first Agents.tsx must be sequenced into one entry so the Studio is built against a single funnel rather than four parallel ones.
- Webhooks router (EWS-16) must relocate/absorb the Phase-0 Razorpay route (FND-20) when it lands, and stay Razorpay-only — do not let the Phase-5 raw-body router grow a Meta inbound handler.

---

## 7. Execution guidance

- **Order is fixed by two gates** (see §5.3): fix the live holes + double-reply (Phase 0, zero schema changes) and get on migrations (Phase 1) before any new table. Then gateway/observability (2) → policy/identity (3) → **spec + creation studio (4)** → event/workflow + outbound (5) → tools + memory (6). Phase 7 is triggered-only.
- **Do-first within Phase 0/1:** QA-01 (isolated test DB + hard prod-DB guard) and the DB migration baseline (DB-1→DB-4) land before anything that touches schema or runs tests, because prod `DATABASE_URL` is live.
- **Every task is additive.** The reply loop, the channel-adapter seam, the training model, and the owner-alert / NEEDS_HUMAN / approve-before-send / {{LINK}} features must not regress — each phase's tests prove it (regression gates: SPEC-10, MGW-18/QA-17, EWS-15/QA-31, TOOL-23, MK-34).
- **"Robust" = UX + backend + architecture together:** a feature is done only when its UX state (empty/loading/error/success) and its test both land, not just the API. UX tasks: 38; Test+Security tests: 150.
- The backlog is now **gap-checked against all three plan docs**. Open decisions remaining are in §5.4 and the per-module open questions (§4).
